## 2026-09-22 — Rebuild Option Book into compact professional order ticket

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Option Book layout / ticket ergonomics.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/option_book_ui.py`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The first two Option Book layouts still behaved like generic Streamlit forms instead of a professional trading ticket. Bid/Ask cards and action controls consumed too much space, leg controls and market data competed for the same row width, the primary Preview action could visually dominate the page, and the saved-drafts table stayed fully open beneath the ticket.
- **Root cause:** The original layout put Action/Qty/Expiration/Strike/Type/Bid/Ask/Remove into one narrow eight-column row and relied on generic Streamlit sizing. That forced market values into tiny columns and made widget/theme defaults determine the visual hierarchy. Pricing also reserved boxes for fields that were not needed by the selected order type.
- **What was changed:** Rebuilt the Option Book presentation structurally. The production renderer now uses one feature-scoped keyed workspace; each option leg is a compact bordered row containing only Action, Qty, Expiration, Strike, Type, and Remove, followed immediately by a full-width six-cell market strip for Bid, Ask, Mid, Spread, Volume, and Open Interest. Pricing now renders only the Stop / Limit / Stop-Limit fields required by the selected price type. The ticket summary is compact, Save Draft and Preview in E*TRADE sit in a right-aligned horizontal action row with feature-owned button styling, the long API disclaimer was reduced to a one-line Preview Only status strip, and saved drafts/previews are collapsed into an expander. Account selection now uses a stable broker/account key; x5474 remains the initial default when available, while a later manual account choice persists.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep Option Book dense and horizontal. Do not put Bid/Ask back into narrow `st.metric` cards. Do not stretch Save/Preview across wide columns. Keep each leg's controls and market strip together. Keep x5474 as the initial default without overriding a valid later manual account selection. Preserve ticker/company autocomplete, live E*TRADE expiration/strike sourcing, 1–4 legs, single-leg stop behavior, spread debit/credit behavior, local drafts, and broker PREVIEW-only safety with no live placement path.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/option_book.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, E*TRADE Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, Holdings, navigation, Bull Debit Spread, Muni tools, legacy Orders, shared theme, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the prior Option Book change history before editing; re-traced the production route `streamlit_app.py → src/option_book_ui.py`; compared the live screenshots against the eight-column implementation and redesigned the information hierarchy around leg controls → market strip → price → review/actions; verified Streamlit's keyed container API exposes a `st-key-<key>` CSS class and supports horizontal containers; re-fetched the exact branch production file after the rewrite; PR #17 Terminal Architecture Guard run `35689132256` executed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` successfully on code commit `0f7b32527d864228c8ba55e0cc1aac1d43565608`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on the production-code head; merge is allowed only after the changelog-only head also passes the guard.
- **Commit SHA:** layout rebuild `560d0c45fd82fd5b10a3f96776f862c8c63e2dfc`; spacing/alignment polish `0f7b32527d864228c8ba55e0cc1aac1d43565608`; final production merge SHA recorded after validation.
- **Lesson:** A trading ticket should not be a stretched generic form. Separate controls from market data, show only fields relevant to the selected order type, use horizontal action groups instead of giant column-width buttons, and scope all visual overrides to the owning feature.



## 2026-09-22 — Restore hidden Risk Sizing Part 2 after two-pane move

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 visibility inside the new Risk Book + trade-sizing side-by-side layout.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** After Part 2 was moved into the right-side pane, the live terminal showed only the Risk Book on the left and a completely blank black area on the right. The Risk Engine caption and Crown notes rendered immediately after the table, confirming the Part 2 widgets existed in the render sequence but their pane was being hidden by CSS.
- **Root cause:** The v10 ticker-row layout CSS used the broad selector `[data-testid="stHorizontalBlock"]:has(.risk-v10-ticker-marker)`. Because the ticker marker sits anywhere inside Part 2, the new OUTER Risk Book + Part 2 horizontal block also matched that selector. The same CSS then hid the outer block's second column, which is the entire Part 2 pane.
- **What was changed:** Narrowed every ticker-row selector so it only matches a horizontal block whose FIRST direct Streamlit column contains `.risk-v10-ticker-marker`: `:has(> [data-testid="stColumn"]:first-child .risk-v10-ticker-marker)`. The inner ticker/quote row still collapses to one full-width ticker search, but ancestor two-column layouts no longer match and therefore cannot hide Part 2.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The Risk Book + Part 2 outer split must stay visible. Only the inner ticker/quote row may collapse its unused second column. Preserve ticker/company autocomplete, automatic E*TRADE quote loading, ASK-seeded Entry, Stop at 5% below ASK, exactly one live stop-distance indicator, and the no-manual-quote-button production behavior. Never use a descendant-wide `:has(.risk-v10-ticker-marker)` selector on `stHorizontalBlock` again because it can match ancestor layouts.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py` two-pane structure, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, risk formulas, ticker directory data, GEX, Option Book, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, Schwab Risk Sizing, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the immediately preceding Risk Sizing layout history; re-traced production path `streamlit_app.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; confirmed the live screenshot symptom matches the CSS failure mode; verified selector semantics against a mock nested Streamlit DOM where only the inner ticker row matches and the outer Risk Book + Part 2 row does not; inspected PR #18 file scope; GitHub Actions `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `35689308491` executed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` successfully.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on code commit `de939735c2a103c9dadbf1255e07e19027b96346`; final guard required after this changelog-only commit.
- **Commit SHA:** selector fix `de939735c2a103c9dadbf1255e07e19027b96346`; final production merge SHA recorded after validation.
- **Lesson:** CSS `:has()` selectors used around Streamlit layout containers must identify the exact intended child relationship. A marker anywhere inside a feature pane is not sufficient because every ancestor layout also contains that marker.

## 2026-09-22 — Option Book design-system audit aligned to E*TRADE Risk Sizing

- **Feature changed:** Option Book typography, spacing, card/control styling, responsive layout, price-seed UX, plus the shared top-level page-header stylesheet delivery.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/option_book_ui.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The deployed Option Book screenshot still looked visually separate from E*TRADE Risk Sizing: internal headers were thin orange text instead of orange/black section bars, control/card sizing used a different rhythm, purple was introduced outside the terminal palette, market/account data used bespoke strip styling instead of Risk-style metric cards, and browser sessions could render the shared page-header markup without its orange title-bar CSS. The limit field could also retain stale pre-audit session seeding instead of reseeding from the current option midpoint once.
- **Root cause:** Option Book had evolved through several local CSS passes instead of using the production Risk Sizing design contract. Separately, `_render_terminal_page_header()` guarded its stylesheet with a process-global `st._raj_page_header_css_installed` flag even though Streamlit output/CSS is browser-session scoped.
- **What was changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing is now the documented canonical Option Book visual reference. Option Book uses the same Courier New hierarchy, 30px orange-filled internal section bars with black text, 38px selects/number inputs/buttons, 52px orange-border metric cards, 4px vertical and 8px horizontal spacing, black surfaces, and green/red/blue financial tones. Underlier/account statistics, option market data, net pricing, and preview totals now share one metric-card system; purple styling was removed. Number-input steppers now use the same explicit orange +/- treatment as Risk Sizing. Responsive metric grids fall to three/two columns instead of colliding. The existing x5474 initial-account default remains intact and manual account changes still persist. The price-seed signature was versioned and now includes the symbol so existing stale sessions reseed the selected contract midpoint once without resetting a user's later manual price edits on normal reruns. The shared top-level page-header stylesheet is now emitted every render/session so all tabs reliably receive the same full-width orange title bar.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing is the visual reference for Option Book, but Risk files must not be edited to style Option Book. Preserve the 30/38/52px sizing contract and 4px/8px grid, x5474 initial default with manual override persistence, 1–4 legs, ticker/company autocomplete, E*TRADE expirations/strikes/quotes, single-leg stop behavior, spread debit/credit behavior, local drafts, and broker PREVIEW-only safety with no live placement path. Shared page-header CSS must be emitted per Streamlit render/session rather than process-global gated.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, Risk formulas, `src/option_book.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, GEX, OAuth, Holdings, Schwab Risk Sizing, top navigation, Bull Debit Spread, Muni, legacy Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

## 2026-09-22 — Refresh All GEX routes disconnected sessions to E*TRADE connect

- **Feature changed:** GEX Refresh All disconnected-session behavior.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** Clicking `REFRESH ALL GEX` while the live E*TRADE session was disconnected could fall through to the legacy synchronous GEX path instead of starting the terminal's normal E*TRADE authorization flow.
- **Root cause:** The proven GEX Refresh All wrapper only owns the connected background-refresh case. When its dedicated background client factory was unavailable, it intentionally delegated to the base button behavior, but there was no GEX-owned bridge from that disconnected click into the existing OAuth request-token start path.
- **What was changed:** Added a GEX-only connection callback in `src/gex_workspace_v2.py` that reuses the same `_etrade_credentials` + `begin_authorization` functions as the existing `CONNECT E*TRADE` control and writes the same `etrade_request` fields. Added a render-scoped `st.button` wrapper in `src/gex_ui_v3.py` that intercepts only `gexv3_refresh_all` while the live client is absent; a click starts authorization and reruns the app so the existing compact E*TRADE verification panel completes the login. When E*TRADE is connected, the wrapper delegates unchanged to the proven Refresh All background path. Visible GEX build bumped to `v2026.09.22.12`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Connected Refresh All must continue using the existing bounded background GEX pipeline, live progress polling, caches, request-rate gate, auto-refresh-on-login behavior, IV Rank, multi-ticker state, and TradingView export. The disconnected bridge must reuse the existing OAuth request/verification flow rather than creating a second login UI. The temporary button wrapper must remain inside the GEX render function and be restored in `finally`.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, navigation, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication logic, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

## 2026-09-22 — Make disconnected Refresh All GEX actually clickable

- **Feature changed:** GEX Refresh All disconnected-session handoff to E*TRADE OAuth.

The click can now enter the already-implemented OAuth handoff. Connected Refresh All remains unchanged. Visible GEX build bumped to `v2026.09.22.13`.

Only the production GEX wrapper may override the disconnected `gexv3_refresh_all` instance, and only while the connect callback exists. Preserve the existing background refresh, rate gate, cache, progress, auto-refresh-on-login, IV Rank, and TradingView behavior.

## 2026-09-22 — Make Refresh All GEX a true connect-or-refresh action

- **Feature changed:** GEX Refresh All E*TRADE authorization handoff.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** After making disconnected Refresh All clickable, the GEX action could create the OAuth request token but did not expose E*TRADE's returned `authorization_url` in the GEX action area, so Raj still had no link to open and obtain the verification code from that button.
- **Root cause:** The existing GEX connect callback stored the normal `etrade_request` payload, including `authorization_url`, but the GEX Refresh All wrapper only toasted and reran. The production OAuth strip owns verification-code entry, but no GEX-owned control rendered the pending authorization URL.
- **What was changed:** The disconnected `gexv3_refresh_all` path now has one action slot with three states. With no live E*TRADE client and no pending request, `REFRESH ALL GEX` creates the existing E*TRADE OAuth request token. On the rerun, if `etrade_request.authorization_url` exists, that same action slot is replaced by `OPEN E*TRADE AUTHORIZATION ↗`, pointing directly to the returned E*TRADE URL. After verification succeeds and a live client exists, the slot automatically returns to the normal `REFRESH ALL GEX` button and delegates unchanged to the existing background GEX refresh path. Visible GEX build bumped to `v2026.09.22.14`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep exactly one primary action in the GEX Refresh All slot. Do not create a parallel GEX login UI or alter OAuth token semantics. The existing compact E*TRADE strip remains responsible for verification-code entry and VERIFY & CONNECT. Connected Refresh All must continue using the existing background refresh, rate gate, caches, live progress, auto-refresh-on-login, IV Rank, multi-ticker state, and TradingView behavior.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/session_persistence.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, navigation, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

- **Lesson:** Starting OAuth is not enough for a one-click GEX handoff; the returned broker authorization URL must be surfaced in the same feature action path. Treat disconnected, pending-authorization, and connected states explicitly so one control can progress from connect → authorize → refresh without duplicating OAuth UI.

## 2026-09-22 — Standardize GEX IV Rank on E*TRADE constant-30-day ATM IV

- **Feature changed:** GEX `IV RANK / REF` source and calculation methodology.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_ui.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken / incomplete:** The existing IV Rank correctly used E*TRADE option IV and retained daily history, but its current-IV observation came from whichever >=30-DTE expiry happened to be inside the user's current GEX max-DTE window. Changing a ticker from 45 DTE to 7 DTE, for example, could change the IV observation definition or force a short-DTE fallback, making the historical series inconsistent. E*TRADE's documented public Market API exposes contract-level `OptionGreeks.iv`, not a ready-made historical IV Rank series.
- **Root cause:** `_representative_iv(...)` operated only on option contracts already fetched for the GEX calculation. The GEX DTE control is a gamma-exposure setting, not an IV-Rank tenor definition, so the IV series could drift with GEX settings.
- **What was changed:** IV Rank now uses a GEX-owned, E*TRADE-only constant-30-day ATM IV observation. The calculation selects the E*TRADE expirations immediately below/above 30 calendar DTE (or exact 30 DTE), extracts ATM call/put `OptionGreeks.iv`, and interpolates implied variance to a constant 30-day tenor. Existing GEX chains are reused whenever they already cover those expirations; a missing bracket is fetched with a compact 12-strike CALLPUT chain rather than changing normal GEX coverage. One standardized observation per ticker/day is retained for 365 days and the existing `(current-low)/(high-low)*100` IV Rank formula remains. Before 10 observations, the column displays current E*TRADE IV plus history progress instead of inventing a rank; after 10 observations it shows a provisional rank with `P` until the stored span reaches roughly one year. The IV cell tooltip identifies E*TRADE `OptionGreeks.iv`, current 30D ATM IV, observation count, and expiry bracket. The history schema is versioned as `ETRADE_30D_ATM_VAR_V1` so older DTE-dependent observations are not mixed into the new series. Visible GEX build bumped to `v2026.09.22.15`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** IV Rank must remain independent of the user's GEX max-DTE setting. Do not scrape Power E*TRADE or use undocumented endpoints as the production source. Preserve GEX wall/flip math, complete eligible GEX expiration coverage, 20 calculation/fetch workers, the shared ~3.7 MARKET request-starts/sec gate, caches, live progress, multi-ticker state, OAuth behavior, auto-refresh-on-login, and TradingView/Pine payload unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/etrade_data_cache.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_proven.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, E*TRADE OAuth UI/session semantics, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, navigation, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the prior GEX/IV Rank history; traced production route `streamlit_app.py → src/gex_workspace_v2.py → src/gex_ui_v3.py → src/gex_ui.py`; verified against E*TRADE's official Market API documentation that option-chain responses expose `OptionGreeks.iv` and that `strikePriceNear` / `noOfStrikes` are documented option-chain parameters, while no documented public historical IV Rank field was found; inspected exact branch code for the 30-DTE bracket, ATM call/put IV extraction, variance interpolation, versioned history reset, column rendering, and unchanged GEX wall/TradingView paths; PR #26 Terminal Architecture Guard run `35776493022` executed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` successfully on code head `a85f84cc0d32a59fd0e25bb40dd0936573526d4d`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on PR #26 run `35776493022`; final changelog-only head requires the guard again before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** initial E*TRADE 30D work `ae4bcd4c731ac178d1f8af403ee7b447e1330e17`; constant-30D variance interpolation `7e1cbd6eadbef21de1635265b2d5905b2ba40150`; IV-source tooltip `c96e75a5497a313af0bdb5baa062e7293b622c2b`; build marker `24adf38b90144008726c700608789aab44a51d55`; history-method versioning `a85f84cc0d32a59fd0e25bb40dd0936573526d4d`.
- **Lesson:** E*TRADE's public API gives the raw IV needed to build IV Rank, not a documented historical IV Rank feed. Standardize the daily E*TRADE IV observation to a fixed tenor before building history; never let a GEX display/control setting redefine the IV Rank series.

## 2026-09-22 — Add researched E*TRADE IV/HV cheap-rich reference to GEX

- **Feature changed:** GEX `IV RANK / REF` methodology, current-IV construction, and IV-vs-30-day-historical-volatility reference.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_ui.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, new `src/gex_realized_vol.py`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken / incomplete:** IV Rank used E*TRADE IV correctly but did not provide an immediate independent reference for whether current option IV was rich or cheap versus how much the stock had actually been moving. The prior standardized 30-day IV also used a simpler ATM average/variance interpolation instead of the documented IVX-style Vega weighting and square-root-DTE interpolation requested for higher methodological fidelity.
- **Root cause:** E*TRADE's documented Market API exposes contract `OptionGreeks.iv` and `OptionGreeks.vega` but no ready-made IV/HV comparison and no documented multi-day historical-close endpoint. A defensible IV/HV metric therefore requires a standardized E*TRADE 30-day IV plus a separately sourced 30-day close-to-close historical volatility series.
- **What was changed:** The current-IV calculation now follows Fidelity's published IVX construction as closely as the documented E*TRADE fields allow: for each of the two expirations bracketing 30 DTE, use the four strikes nearest spot on each side, Vega-weight E*TRADE `OptionGreeks.iv`, interpolate call and put IV separately to 30 days linearly in square-root DTE, then average call/put 30-day IV for the mean. IV Rank history is versioned as `ETRADE_IVX30_VEGA_SQRT_V2`, so older differently constructed observations are not mixed in, and the `P` marker now remains until the stored series spans a full 365 calendar days. The new GEX-only historical-volatility module calculates HV30 from 30 daily log returns `ln(Pt/Pt-1)`, sample standard deviation using `n-1`, annualized by `sqrt(252)`. With an authorized server-side `TIINGO_API_TOKEN`, it loads Tiingo's documented adjusted daily closes; without an authorized token or sufficient history, it returns N/A instead of fabricating a value. The reference is `IV/HV % = current 30D IV / HV30 * 100`: above 100% is `RICH`, below 100% is `CHEAP`, exactly 100% is `FAIR`. The column shows IV Rank on the first compact line and IV/HV on the second. A `?` hover/focus control in the `IV RANK / REF` header explains both formulas, every variable, the `P` marker, sources, and the limitation that rich/cheap is relative rather than proof of mispricing. Visible GEX build is `v2026.09.22.16`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep E*TRADE as the authoritative option-IV/vega source. Keep IV Rank independent of the user's GEX max-DTE setting. Never scrape Power E*TRADE or fabricate missing HV. The historical-price credential must remain server-side and must be licensed appropriately for the deployment. Preserve GEX wall/flip math, eligible-expiration coverage, 20 calculation/fetch workers, shared ~3.7 E*TRADE MARKET request-start gate, caches, live progress, multi-ticker state, Refresh All OAuth behavior, auto-refresh-on-login, and TradingView/Pine output unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`, `src/session_persistence.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_proven.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, navigation, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Research basis:** E*TRADE's official Market API documents `OptionGreeks.iv` and `OptionGreeks.vega`; Fidelity's IV Index guide documents four nearest strikes, Vega weighting, two expirations around 30 days, square-root-DTE interpolation, 30-day historical volatility from close-to-close log returns with `n-1`, and the direct `current IV / 30-day HV` percentage; CME documents annualized log-return realized volatility using 252 trading days; Schwab documents the all-else-equal rich/cheap interpretation of IV above/below historical volatility; Tiingo documents adjusted closes and split/dividend adjustments. The implementation deliberately calls this IV/HV, not volatility risk premium, because the latter normally compares implied volatility with subsequent realized volatility.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and prior GEX/IV Rank change history; traced production route `streamlit_app.py → src/gex_workspace_v2.py → src/gex_ui_v3.py → src/gex_ui.py`; inspected exact branch code for four-nearest-contract selection, Vega weighting, square-root-DTE interpolation, call/put mean, 30 log-return sample standard deviation, `sqrt(252)` annualization, exact 100% reference threshold, N/A failure behavior, server-only token handling, compact two-line cell, and `?` hover/focus help; checked the published 12/47-DTE Fidelity example gives approximately 40.6%/59.4% interpolation weights, consistent with its rounded 0.4/0.6 example; PR #27 Terminal Architecture Guard run `35779767082` executed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` successfully and syntax-checked the staged Python. No live Tiingo request was claimed because no deployment token is available in the GitHub connector environment.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on PR #27 run `35779767082` for code head `db313ca607efbdd15e663aa2b7e6db707fe55aa9`; final changelog-only head must pass again before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** historical-volatility module `7111c972c16959c897ac27b202ed7f85a48364d1`; E*TRADE IVX30 + IV/HV calculation `8b8ba1f468fc9a8b5b5d3dedbe7533b384ce7997`; overview/help UI `dc52afc009da46f89dce0e35d11560ac622c8f23` + `71850caa1bcdef1c5c496682308177f12ca19319`; historical-source plumbing/build `916098a647cc00a27721dd01b4c120cb3e3c3c0e`; architecture map `473d32205d4a20ddb7b1f277caa770901bacceda`; exact ratio/display hardening `579e550a0cb7f3c8445d32509fa4c208a157a354` + `3e5ac1a505bd5c9f9bc807d7116f45af742829b6`; N/A/help hardening `1eba8c1c7b243fddac08430767555994a96db502`; full-year IV Rank gate `db313ca607efbdd15e663aa2b7e6db707fe55aa9`.
- **Lesson:** For a defensible option-richness reference, standardize the implied-volatility tenor and construction first, then compare it with an equivalently annualized trailing historical-volatility window. Keep the comparison labeled as relative IV/HV rather than claiming true mispricing, and never hide missing/licensing-dependent historical data behind a guessed value.

## 2026-09-23 — Remove subtitle overlap and remember editable per-ticker DTE

- **Feature changed:** Shared header geometry and GEX per-ticker DTE editing/persistence.
- **Exact production files changed:** `streamlit_app.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_ui.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`. Documentation: architecture map and this log. Tests: `scripts/test_gex_dte.py`, `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`.
- **What was broken:** The GEX build badge overlapped the page subtitle. Overview DTE cells were static; legacy state cleaning discarded saved DTE values outside preset choices, preventing exact broker expiration values from surviving sessions.
- **Root cause:** Live DOM measurement showed Streamlit's Markdown wrapper had a -16px bottom margin: the title/subtitle content was 45.75px tall while its parent reserved only 29.75px. The legacy cleaner accepted only 7/14/21/30/45/etc. overrides, and background prefetch treated 0 as a missing value.
- **What changed:** Render the shared title/subtitle with `st.html` instead of Markdown. Each Overview DTE opens a compact ticker-specific numeric editor; Save loads that ticker's E*TRADE expiration dates, snaps to the nearest nonexpired DTE (earlier on ties), and refreshes only that ticker before committing its setting/results. Exact integer overrides, including 0DTE, persist through session state, the existing vault, and browser storage. Settings displays the exact override. Background refresh blocks concurrent DTE edits; lookup/calculation errors preserve the old setting/result. GEX build `v2026.09.23.1`.
- **Important behavior to preserve:** DTE remains a maximum window including all eligible expirations up to the selected cutoff, not a single-expiry-only calculation. Retain per-ticker preferences across reloads and app restarts via existing browser storage; do not coerce nonpreset values back to global/preset values. Do not change other tickers when saving. Keep fixed-30D IV Rank independent of the GEX cutoff. Browser storage must remain available for cross-session recovery; this is not a new cross-device database.
- **Intentionally unchanged:** Navigation component/layout spacing, Risk Sizing and math, OAuth/login UI, Holdings, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, E*TRADE client transport, GEX formulas/IV/HV methodology, TradingView, and `src/terminal_core.py`. The shared app edit is necessary because it owns the under-measured page heading.
- **Tests performed:** Traced all production routes; Python compile/diff checks; architecture/workflow guards. Four automated DTE tests cover nearest/exact/tie/out-of-range/expired/empty dates, nonpreset/zero state round trips, invalid values, selected-ticker isolation, API failure rollback, disconnected/running guards, and table/query preservation. AppTest rendered all eight tabs plus repeated GEX/Risk and a second session with one shared heading each. Browser local full-app test with synthetic E*TRADE responses: 22 entered → 23 saved, QQQ retained 45, refreshed table and Settings show 23, page reload preserves 23, and full server-process restart restores 23 from browser storage. Measured a 4px gap below the complete subtitle and visually checked separation after restart. Broker transport was simulated locally; no production DTE values were altered in testing. Inspected exact committed production changes.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS locally; GitHub guard required before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** `7858b57be7fc7da7454acdefefe71246b8e7f1f8` (implementation/tests).
- **Lesson:** Test the bottom of the complete heading, not only its title's top edge. Persistent preference schemas must accept real broker values; a saved control is not persistent if a legacy cleaner silently drops it on reload.


## 2026-09-23 — Add isolated CBOE GEX Overview and saved MASTER A6 source

- **Feature changed:** GEX source selection, CBOE delayed-options GEX, Overview subtabs, and TradingView MASTER A6 source routing.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** new `src/gex_cboe.py`; `src/gex_ui_v3.py`; `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`; `src/gex_ui.py` for persisted source-selection state only; `src/gex_github_bridge.py`; ownership documentation in `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Regression coverage in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`; `scripts/validate_architecture.py` now syntax-checks all production GEX source files.
- **What was broken / missing:** The production GEX workspace had one E*TRADE-backed Overview/result path and one MASTER A6 bridge source. Raj could not view the original CBOE delayed-options GEX beside E*TRADE or choose which source supplied the TradingView MASTER A6 payload.
- **Root cause:** The current production renderer and bridge were source-specific to E*TRADE. The prior CBOE exporter lived outside the production terminal path, and there was no persisted GEX source preference or source-isolated CBOE result cache/bridge.
- **What was changed:** Added an isolated CBOE delayed-options engine using the original CBOE delayed quote option-chain endpoints and OCC parsing, while matching the production E*TRADE wall/flip calculation definitions so source comparisons do not also change methodology. The GEX workspace now has `E*TRADE OVERVIEW` and `CBOE OVERVIEW` subtabs. CBOE refresh supports all saved tickers or one ticker, uses up to eight bounded fetch workers, has its own process cache/failure state, and renders the same compact Overview geometry/range visualization without mixing E*TRADE IV Rank into the CBOE source view. TradingView now has a saved `MASTER A6 SOURCE` selector for `CBOE` or `E*TRADE`; CBOE is the initial default and the last selection persists through the existing GEX state/browser-storage path. Existing E*TRADE MASTER A6 continues publishing to `bridge/latest_gex.txt`. CBOE publishes independently to `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`, so CBOE refreshes cannot overwrite the E*TRADE bridge.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep E*TRADE and CBOE result maps, refresh paths, and GitHub bridge files source-isolated. Do not change E*TRADE wall/flip formulas, IV Rank/IV-HV construction, OAuth/session plumbing, request-rate gate, background Refresh All, DTE persistence, multi-ticker state, or current `latest_gex.txt` semantics when changing CBOE. Keep CBOE as the default MASTER A6 source only when no prior source preference exists; once the user chooses CBOE or E*TRADE, preserve that choice. CBOE Overview must not silently display E*TRADE IV Rank as if it were CBOE-derived.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and prior GEX history; traced production route `streamlit_app.py → src/gex_workspace_v2.py → src/gex_ui_v3.py → src/gex_ui_v3_proven.py → src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`; reviewed the saved original CBOE exporter/schema; inspected the exact branch production code and changed-file boundary. Temporary CI run `35878894866` installed production requirements, successfully byte-compiled `src/gex_cboe.py`, `src/gex_github_bridge.py`, `src/gex_ui.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, and `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`; passed `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py` with mocked CBOE payloads; re-passed `scripts/test_gex_dte.py`; and `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` rendered all eight production top-level tabs without exceptions. Terminal Architecture Guard run `35878895137` also passed on the final production-code head. Separate live-source smoke run `35879099706` successfully fetched the current CBOE SPY delayed-options endpoint and verified `12,380` option rows plus a usable underlying spot from `https://cdn.cboe.com/api/global/delayed_quotes/options/SPY.json`; no broker credentials were used.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on production-code head `d7f505a143ddb95f12d697264308705e292053c1`; final changelog-only head must pass again before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** isolated CBOE engine `f1b5e410645e1c6c22ea526f95e6839ab0af6117`; source-state persistence `d4932274842e019a07b132cd02371bf6a932a7c2`; separate bridge publisher `cd9301ebc076c54021ad2685276fb7d1fdf998b3`; Overview/source-routing production code culminates at `d7f505a143ddb95f12d697264308705e292053c1`; final merge SHA recorded after validation.
- **Lesson:** Compare broker/data sources with one calculation definition and separate result/transport state. Do not overwrite the established E*TRADE bridge or mix E*TRADE-derived reference metrics into a CBOE-labeled source view.


## 2026-09-23 — Keep E*TRADE RENEW button fixed in place

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE session-renew interaction / connection-strip layout stability.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** Clicking `RENEW` displayed the success message inside the same narrow centered Streamlit column as the button. The column became taller after the click, so Streamlit vertically re-centered the button and the entire RENEW control visibly jumped.
- **Root cause:** `st.success("E*TRADE session renewed.")` lived inside `renew_col` while the five-column connection row used `vertical_alignment="center"`. Feedback content therefore changed the geometry of the control row.
- **What was changed:** Successful renewal now stores a one-shot renewal notice, reruns immediately so the session timer reflects the renewed session, and displays the confirmation as a non-layout `st.toast` on the rerun. Renew errors also use a toast instead of inserting a variable-height error box into the RENEW column. No button sizing, column ratios, OAuth controls, or renewal API semantics were changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The RENEW button must stay at the same position and size before/after a click. Renewal must still call the existing live E*TRADE client's `renew()`, then the existing session-touch callback. CONNECT, DISCONNECT, LOCK, verification-code input, and VERIFY & CONNECT behavior remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/session_persistence.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, GEX, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and shared theme.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and traced the production route `streamlit_app.py → src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`. Terminal Architecture Guard run `35880545190` passed on implementation head `e93b1c84348a045438767e6d123fb3a9073ad93a`. Temporary CI run `35880672704` installed production requirements, byte-compiled `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py` and `streamlit_app.py`, verified the renew-layout source contract, and ran `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` with all production top-level tabs rendering successfully. The temporary workflow was then deleted so it is not part of the production diff.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on final PR head `757c4b11708ab6847407fa38c24cc38cb7f31423` in Terminal Architecture Guard run `35880857085`.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `e93b1c84348a045438767e6d123fb3a9073ad93a`.
- **Lesson:** Never render variable-height success/error boxes inside a vertically centered compact control column. Use non-layout feedback or a separate row so interaction feedback cannot move the control itself.


## 2026-09-23 — Align E*TRADE and CBOE GEX Overview layout

- **Feature changed:** GEX source-view layout consistency and CBOE DTE interaction parity.

The CBOE DTE value was also static while the E*TRADE DTE value opened the saved ticker DTE editor.

CBOE per-ticker refresh uses the same visible labels/geometry as E*TRADE, and its DTE cell now opens the same persisted per-ticker DTE editor. The separate CBOE bridge continues publishing internally to `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`; only the inconsistent raw transport link was removed from the Overview UI.

Keep `bridge/latest_gex.txt` for E*TRADE and `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt` for CBOE. Keep CBOE as the initial MASTER A6 source only when no prior preference exists. Do not mix E*TRADE IV Rank / IV-HV into CBOE results. Both source tabs should follow the same table geometry, DTE editing behavior, spacing, and refresh-control styling.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py`, `src/gex_ui.py` GEX math/IV logic, `src/gex_github_bridge.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_proven.py`, `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Risk Sizing, Schwab Risk Sizing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

Terminal Architecture Guard run `35882635717` passed on the production-code head. Temporary CI run `35882694935` installed production requirements, byte-compiled `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`, and `streamlit_app.py`; verified both source refresh controls and removal of the one-off CBOE bridge/explainer UI; passed `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`; passed `scripts/test_gex_dte.py`; and rendered all production top-level tabs via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`. The temporary workflow was removed afterward.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on production-code head `c0f2a54904fe577c55c7b2c3b031491ecd3fc5f7` in run `35882635717`; the final PR head is revalidated before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** shared source controls `09b479ee85bd46a186a7677839f3c50b952cab7f` + `a2800cf59dc2ab028d0f37b31d39c518f1676dab`; source-tab consistency `d401279a576c33c84c4b3c0d3063d3515b4747ac`; production hook wiring `c0f2a54904fe577c55c7b2c3b031491ecd3fc5f7`.

## 2026-09-23 — Add one-off LONG-TERM intent override to E*TRADE Risk Book

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing per-holding classification intent.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken / missing:** The Risk Book classification was entirely automatic: bonds/CUSIPs were LONG-TERM and other holdings were LONG-TERM only when Gain/Loss % met the global profit threshold. That meant a position such as gold at -5% was forced into TACTICAL even when Raj intended to own it as a long-term structural holding.
- **Root cause:** The base Risk Book had no per-ticker intent/override state between the automatic `classify_holdings(...)` result and `sleeve_summary(...)`.
- **What was changed:** Added an account-scoped `INTENT` editor column to the Risk Book with `AUTO` and `LONG-TERM`. `AUTO` preserves the existing bond/CUSIP + gain-threshold rule. `LONG-TERM` promotes only that selected ticker to LONG-TERM before sleeve totals are calculated, labels its rule `MANUAL LONG-TERM INTENT`, removes its absolute market value from CURRENT TACTICAL, adds it to LONG-TERM / STRUCTURAL, and increases TACTICAL ROOM by the same amount. Returning the row to `AUTO` removes the manual override. The edit state is scoped by selected E*TRADE account in the current terminal session so one account's manual intent does not leak into another account.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The automatic classification rule remains the default for every ticker. Manual intent may only promote a ticker to LONG-TERM; it does not rewrite the global gain threshold or force other holdings to change. Existing bonds/CUSIPs remain structural automatically. Part 2 sizing formulas, Crown risk budget, ticker/company autocomplete, automatic E*TRADE quote loading, ASK-seeded Entry, 5%-below-ASK Stop, one live stop-distance indicator, and the Risk Book + Part 2 side-by-side layout must remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/ticker_autocomplete.py`, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and prior Risk Sizing history; traced `streamlit_app.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`. Temporary CI run `35899839628` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the full Risk production path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py` using a GLD -5% example, verified Streamlit's `data_editor` / `SelectboxColumn` contract, verified the production source wiring, and rendered all production top-level tabs through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`. The temporary workflow was removed afterward.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on the production implementation via Terminal Architecture Guard run `35899726770`; final PR head is revalidated after changelog/test-harness commits before merge.
- **Commit SHA:** production UI `e37159b414873efe9f4ec4451d866d1fe2ad1db5`; regression test `f91a5b56fad0c2f94a74cb2aaa7fbda3a35a4392` + import-path fix `d6550b9728c3a1db6a4624780c59d505537e0f36`.
- **Lesson:** Portfolio intent is not always inferable from current P&L. Keep the automatic classification as the baseline, but allow a narrowly scoped manual LONG-TERM promotion before sleeve totals are calculated rather than changing the portfolio-wide threshold.


## 2026-09-23 — Restore Risk Book colors after LONG-TERM intent editor

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book presentation after adding the per-ticker LONG-TERM override.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** After the editable intent column was added, normal Risk Book body cells such as Symbol, Market Value, and % Account rendered black on the black table body, and the editable INTENT dropdown could also disappear visually. The existing red/green P&L and blue Tactical Sleeve cells remained visible, which made the regression inconsistent across columns.
- **Root cause:** The original Risk Book used `st.dataframe`, which is wrapped by the terminal dataframe theme to default unstyled body cells to Bloomberg orange. The new `st.data_editor` bypassed that `st.dataframe` wrapper. As a result, any cell without an explicit Styler color inherited the app's black `textColor`. Editable selectbox cells also do not reliably honor Pandas Styler text colors.
- **What was changed:** The Risk Book row Styler now explicitly seeds every non-financial body cell with `BB_ORANGE`, then preserves the existing sleeve/P&L/tactical-sleeve overrides (orange/green, red/green, blue). The text-based `INTENT` dropdown was replaced with an editable `LONG-TERM` checkbox column, keeping the one-off override behavior while avoiding unreadable black editable text inside the grid. Checked = manual LONG-TERM; unchecked = existing automatic rule.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Normal Risk Book body text must remain orange on black. Gain/Loss and Gain/Loss % stay red/green by sign; % Tactical Sleeve stays blue; Tactical/LONG-TERM sleeve labels keep their existing orange/green meaning. The manual LONG-TERM override must continue to move only the selected ticker out of Current Tactical and into Long-Term / Structural. Part 2 sizing behavior and all Risk formulas remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/theme.py`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the immediately preceding Risk intent/Risk Book formatting history; traced production path `streamlit_app.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; inspected the exact committed production file. Temporary CI run `35900799864` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the full Risk production path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, verified the orange/default + red/green/blue color contract and checkbox intent column, and rendered every production top-level tab via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation/temp-CI head in run `35900804844`; final PR head is revalidated after removing the temporary workflow and appending this changelog entry.

- **Lesson:** When replacing a themed `st.dataframe` with `st.data_editor`, do not assume the dataframe wrapper/theme still applies. Explicitly preserve the table's body-color contract, and avoid text editors inside a dark grid when the editable renderer cannot honor the existing Styler colors.


## 2026-09-23 — Fix LONG-TERM checkbox refresh loop and keep checkbox outline orange

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing one-off LONG-TERM override interaction and local checkbox appearance.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** Checking a ticker such as SGOL as LONG-TERM could repeatedly rerun/refresh the page. The unchecked LONG-TERM checkbox outline also did not match the terminal orange theme.
- **Root cause:** The LONG-TERM checkbox lived inside `st.data_editor`, whose edit state is row-position based. Once a checked ticker changed from TACTICAL to LONG-TERM, the Risk Book re-sorted that ticker into a different row while the editor still held the old row edit state, allowing the edit to be reapplied against a different row and trigger additional reruns. Streamlit's dataframe checkbox is canvas-rendered, so its unchecked outline cannot be safely recolored with feature-local CSS without changing shared theme primitives.
- **What was changed:** Restored the Risk Book itself to the original static `st.dataframe` presentation. Added a narrow native Streamlit LONG-TERM checkbox column directly beside the table. Every checkbox is keyed by E*TRADE account + ticker and updates only that ticker through an `on_change` callback; there is no manual `st.rerun()` in the override path. Because state follows the symbol instead of the row number, re-sorting SGOL after classification cannot create a refresh loop. The native checkbox column has Risk-Sizing-local CSS that forces its unchecked outline and selected state to Bloomberg orange.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Checking a ticker produces one normal Streamlit rerun and then stops. The selected ticker moves to LONG-TERM / STRUCTURAL and leaves Current Tactical; unchecking returns it to the normal automatic rule. Risk Book body colors remain the original orange/red/green/blue contract. The checkbox outline remains orange. Do not alter shared terminal theme files to solve this feature-local control.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/theme.py`, `.streamlit/config.toml`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the two immediately preceding Risk Book intent/color entries; traced production route `streamlit_app.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`. Temporary CI run `35901641730` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the full Risk path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, verified there is no `st.data_editor` in this Risk path, verified the symbol-keyed native `st.checkbox` callback and feature-local orange border CSS, and rendered all production top-level tabs via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation head via Terminal Architecture Guard run `35901622944`; final PR head is revalidated after the temporary workflow removal and changelog append.
- **Commit SHA:** production implementation `bf4999ae08f122d531e20df61d355415e112392f`; callback regression update `88aa68600019d863c519406d75e334fbd74c88a1`.
- **Lesson:** Do not change the shared Raj's Terminal theme for a feature-local visual request unless Raj explicitly asks for a global theme change. Keep Risk Book overrides keyed to account+ticker, never to row position, because classification changes can re-sort the table.

## 2026-09-23 — Re-implement LONG-TERM checkbox with duplicate-row-safe keys

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book one-off LONG-TERM selector after emergency rollback.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The first native-checkbox retry (PR #39) crashed portfolios containing more than one Risk Book row for the same symbol because every checkbox used the same account+symbol Streamlit key. The earlier editable-grid version also repeatedly reran when a checked ticker re-sorted from TACTICAL to LONG-TERM.
- **Root cause:** Symbol alone is not a unique rendered-row identity, while the editable dataframe held checkbox edits by row position and then changed row order after classification.
- **What was changed:** Kept the original static themed Risk Book dataframe and added the LONG-TERM checkbox controls locally beside it. Each checkbox now uses a guaranteed row-unique key composed from account + source-row id + symbol, while the actual override remains ticker-level. Duplicate rows/lots for the same ticker are synchronized from the one ticker-level override before each widget is instantiated. User interaction uses only Streamlit's normal single rerun through `on_change`; there is no manual `st.rerun()` in the override path. The checkbox outline and selected state are styled orange only inside the Risk Book override container.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Duplicate holdings for SGOL or any other ticker must never trigger `StreamlitDuplicateElementKey`. Checking one duplicate row applies the LONG-TERM intent to the ticker and synchronizes its other rows; unchecking removes the override. Risk Book normal text/colors remain the pre-editor theme. Do not change `src/theme.py` or `.streamlit/config.toml` for this feature.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/theme.py`, `.streamlit/config.toml`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, Part 2 quote/sizing logic, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, OAuth, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Started from the post-rollback production main. Temporary CI run `35903107014` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the full E*TRADE Risk path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed the Risk intent regression, and ran a real Streamlit `AppTest` with two SGOL rows to prove the checkbox keys are distinct, checking one synchronizes both without an exception/rerun loop, and unchecking returns both to false. The same run verified the feature-local orange checkbox CSS and rendered all production top-level tabs through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation head via Terminal Architecture Guard; final head is revalidated after removing temporary CI and appending this entry.
- **Commit SHA:** production implementation `c80525544b49a5784d1ba631ed97c07d0caa6848` + row-id hardening `7d40ae896d9ecb1cdae09e634ce9413fdcedcc61`; duplicate-symbol regression `1ba5687eec0ccb19e196dc335ac0335c7144634d` + Streamlit AppTest `42a9183cd8336983250ba87019662f978fe9ee35`.
- **Lesson:** A ticker can appear in multiple portfolio rows/lots. Rendered widget identity must be row-unique, while portfolio intent can remain ticker-level; synchronize duplicate widgets from the shared ticker intent instead of sharing a Streamlit key.


## 2026-09-23 — Align E*TRADE Risk Book LONG-TERM grid after checkbox retry

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book layout/theme alignment only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The safe native LONG-TERM checkbox retry worked functionally, but the checkbox column rendered as a separate visual block: its header/body rows were vertically offset from the Risk Book dataframe, the checkbox column had different spacing/borders, and the dataframe was still content-sized so the final columns could clip against the Part 2 pane.
- **Root cause:** Native Streamlit checkbox element containers retained their own wrapper height/spacing while the dataframe used its own row geometry. The two columns were separated by Streamlit column gap, and the dataframe used `width="content"` plus a capped height, so it was not guaranteed to consume only the available left-pane width or stay vertically synchronized with the checkbox rows.
- **What was changed:** Scoped a `risk_book_grid` container around the checkbox/dataframe pair. The checkbox header and every checkbox cell are fixed to 35px, element-container gaps/margins are removed, the checkbox cells use black backgrounds with orange grid borders and orange checkbox outlines, and the checkbox/dataframe column gap is collapsed locally. The Risk Book dataframe now uses `width="stretch"`, `row_height=35`, compact column widths, and an uncapped exact height of one 35px header plus one 35px row per holding so there is no independent internal scroll to drift away from the checkbox column.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep the original Risk Book financial colors and shared terminal theme untouched. LONG-TERM checkboxes remain account+row+symbol unique, duplicate ticker rows synchronize to one ticker-level intent, and there is still no manual `st.rerun()` in the override callback. Part 2 stays in the right pane.

- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on the production implementation via Terminal Architecture Guard; final PR head is revalidated after temporary CI removal and this changelog append.
- **Commit SHA:** Risk Book grid implementation `14bb07b819c31e3963345e7e776f0886a2a69d49`.
- **Lesson:** A feature-local control next to a dataframe must share the dataframe's exact row geometry and available-width contract. Do not rely on Streamlit's default widget wrapper spacing when visual row alignment matters.

## 2026-09-23 — Restore integrated E*TRADE Risk Book LONG-TERM controls

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book layout, per-holding LONG-TERM intent interaction, and compact sleeve presentation.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The prior safe native-checkbox implementation rendered LONG-TERM controls as a separate strip beside the Risk Book dataframe. The strip and dataframe used different rendering primitives, so their headers/rows could visually drift, the table was squeezed, and the Risk Book no longer looked like one cohesive terminal grid. Raj also wanted Sleeve and % Tactical Sleeve combined to reclaim width.
- **Root cause:** Native Streamlit checkbox widgets and the dataframe canvas do not share one row-geometry system. Trying to force both to 35px with feature-local CSS could not make them a true single grid. The older editable-grid attempt had a different failure: it sorted rows by the effective Sleeve after an edit, while Streamlit editor state is row-position based, so a checked row could move and stale edit state could be reapplied.
- **What was changed:** Replaced the detached checkbox strip with one integrated `st.data_editor` Risk Book. Only the LONG-TERM checkbox column is editable. The display order is now anchored to each holding's automatic classification before manual overrides, so checking a ticker does not move its editor row. A versioned, account-scoped editor key remounts the editor after a committed checkbox change, clearing stale row-position edits. The actual override remains ticker-level, so duplicate lots of the same ticker stay synchronized. `Sleeve` and `% Tactical Sleeve` are now one compact `SLEEVE / % TACTICAL` column: tactical rows show `TACTICAL // xx.xx%`; long-term rows show `LONG-TERM`. The original side-by-side Risk Book + Part 2 geometry, 35px table rows, black/orange terminal treatment, and red/green P&L meaning are preserved.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Checking LONG-TERM must update the classified holdings before `sleeve_summary(...)`, so CURRENT TACTICAL, LONG-TERM / STRUCTURAL, TACTICAL ROOM, and downstream next-trade capacity use the new classification on the normal Streamlit rerun. Unchecking returns that ticker to the automatic bond/CUSIP + gain-threshold rule. Duplicate ticker lots share one ticker-level intent. Do not sort the editor by the post-override Sleeve, do not add a manual `st.rerun()` loop, and do not reintroduce a detached native-checkbox column.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/ticker_autocomplete.py`, shared theme files, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the complete Risk intent/rollback history; traced the production import path. GitHub Actions Temporary Risk Book Validation run `35905569887` on implementation head `a9bbf2d6f1ce87c799af0a938e2b2405a01b8de9` installed production requirements, byte-compiled `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py`, and `streamlit_app.py`; ran `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; and rendered all production top-level tabs with `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`. The temporary validation workflow was removed after it passed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation head via Terminal Architecture Guard run `35905575056`; final PR head is revalidated after this changelog append.
- **Commit SHA:** integrated Risk Book implementation `c1a3100c87765c1bd28d5fe45c20efed0098f538`; editor-state hardening `a9bbf2d6f1ce87c799af0a938e2b2405a01b8de9`.
- **Lesson:** When an editable table's state is keyed by row position, keep its visual order stable across the edit and explicitly discard stale edit state after committing the change. Do not bolt native widgets beside a canvas-rendered table when pixel-level row alignment is required.

## 2026-09-23 — Make E*TRADE Risk Book LONG-TERM checkboxes visibly interactive

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book LONG-TERM control visibility and interaction safety.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** After the integrated Risk Book editor deployed, the LONG-TERM column occupied the correct space but its checkbox controls were effectively invisible against the black table body. The screenshot showed a blank first column even though the boolean editor cells were present.
- **Root cause:** `st.data_editor` boolean cells are rendered inside Streamlit's canvas-based data grid. Raj's Terminal intentionally sets global dataframe `textColor` to black so dataframe headers remain black on Bloomberg orange; that same grid theme makes the boolean checkbox outline/check mark disappear against black body cells. Feature-local DOM CSS cannot reliably recolor pixels drawn inside the dataframe canvas.
- **What was changed:** Replaced only the Risk Book's canvas editor with one feature-local native Streamlit grid built from the same 35px row geometry. The LONG-TERM control is now a real `st.checkbox` inside the first cell of every row, with a 2px Bloomberg-orange outline, orange checked fill, black check mark, and hover treatment. All Risk Book cells use the same native row/column geometry, so the checkbox is not a detached strip. `SLEEVE / % TACTICAL` stays combined, tactical percentages remain blue, LONG-TERM labels green, P&L red/green, and other financial values orange. Widget keys are account + source-row + symbol unique, while the actual override remains ticker-level so duplicate lots stay synchronized.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Checking a ticker must update the ticker-level LONG-TERM override through the normal Streamlit callback rerun, then recalculate CURRENT TACTICAL, LONG-TERM / STRUCTURAL, TACTICAL ROOM, and Part 2 capacity. Unchecking returns the ticker to the normal automatic rule. Duplicate lots must never share a widget key or raise `StreamlitDuplicateElementKey`. Do not reintroduce the canvas `CheckboxColumn` while global dataframe text remains black, do not use a manual `st.rerun()` loop, and do not change shared theme/config for this Risk-only problem.

- **Lesson:** When an interactive dataframe control is canvas-rendered, feature-local CSS cannot safely fix its internal drawing colors. For a Risk-only control that must match a black/orange terminal theme, keep the interaction native and scope the entire grid locally instead of changing the shared dataframe theme.

## 2026-09-23 — Normalize E*TRADE Risk Book typography and grid spacing

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book visual consistency only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regression coverage in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; documentation in `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md`.
- **What was broken:** The functional native LONG-TERM grid still looked visually inconsistent with the rest of the Risk tab. The Risk Book used ad-hoc `.62rem` header text and `.66rem` body text, a compressed LONG-TERM column, and `st.columns(..., gap="small")` while CSS separately tried to erase Streamlit's default gap. The screenshot therefore showed tiny table typography and a cramped header compared with the established Risk controls and section bars.
- **Root cause:** The checkbox visibility repair introduced local styling values instead of reusing the existing production Risk visual contract in `risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`. The project already standardizes Courier New, 35px compact data rows, 8px horizontal rhythm, orange/black headers, and consistent financial colors. Streamlit also natively supports `gap=None` for zero-gap columns and `width="stretch"` for checkboxes, so overriding the default column gap in CSS was unnecessary.
- **What was changed:** Added local Risk Book design tokens: 35px row height, one `.70rem` table font size for both header and body text, and 8px cell padding. Header/body typography now use the same Courier New family, numeric cells use tabular figures, and the LONG-TERM column was widened so its heading is readable without shrinking the font. Header and body rows now call `st.columns(..., gap=None)` directly, and each native LONG-TERM checkbox uses `width="stretch"` so it fills its grid cell cleanly. The established `.risk-v9-section` section-bar styling remains untouched at `.78rem`; the Risk Book now follows it instead of defining a competing header system.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep the Risk Book and Part 2 side-by-side. Keep the LONG-TERM checkboxes visible and duplicate-row safe. Preserve the combined `SLEEVE / % TACTICAL` column, 35px body rows, orange/black terminal language, green/red/blue financial meaning, ticker-level override math, and normal Streamlit callback reruns. Do not change shared theme/config to style this feature.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `.streamlit/config.toml`, `src/theme.py`, ticker autocomplete, Part 2 quote/sizing behavior, Schwab Risk Sizing, GEX, OAuth, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and Risk Book fix history; traced `streamlit_app.py → risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → v9/v2`. Reviewed current Streamlit API guidance for native zero-gap columns, stretched checkbox width, and style-only `st.html`. Temporary GitHub Actions run `35909073442` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the complete E*TRADE Risk production path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, passed the duplicate-safe checkbox + typography regression, and rendered every production top-level tab via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in validation run `35909073442`; final PR head is revalidated after this changelog append.
- **Commit SHA:** typography/grid implementation `bba74a67f41fdc43b180cac337fced37cc30d501`; regression contract `1afb27326cc991430cf6cf20306be3c8246cd237`.
- **Lesson:** Feature-local UI repairs must reuse the production feature's existing design tokens instead of inventing smaller font sizes or compensating for native layout defaults with CSS. Prefer Streamlit's own `gap=None` and `width="stretch"` contracts where they express the intended layout directly.

## 2026-09-23 — Readable E*TRADE Risk Book and consistent Part 2 controls

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing presentation only.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`. Tests: `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** The live Risk Book and supplied screenshot showed tiny table/header text, disconnected row borders, and compressed Part 2 labels/cards. Prior typography normalization still used 11.2px table text versus 16.32px workspace metric values.
- **Root cause:** The native grid retained a 16px parent gap because its CSS targeted a child VerticalBlock instead of the keyed VerticalBlock itself. Cells used 35px HTML inside Markdown wrappers with a measured -16px bottom margin. Part 2 also mixed Markdown geometry with fixed-height cards and selectbox CSS targeting only the older BaseWeb markup, while live Streamlit uses React Aria groups.
- **What changed:** Table body, table headings and internal section headings now share 1.02rem Courier New (16.32px at the tested root size). Rows are 56px with 6px vertical / 8px horizontal padding, native `gap=None`, soft amber row separators, and no individual vertical cell borders. HTML cells, section bars, metric cards and the stop badge use `st.html` to avoid Markdown under-measurement. The combined sleeve column wraps without shrinking type. Removed the empty right spacer and gave the table a 1.2:1 desktop split. A keyed Risk-only container stacks the two panes below 1300px available width. Part 2 labels use consistent left alignment, 8px gaps and 38px control shells; cards fit larger labels with a 60px minimum and grow with content. Both current React Aria and older BaseWeb selectboxes are covered locally.
- **Behavior to preserve:** Native orange LONG-TERM checkboxes; row-unique account/source-row/symbol keys; ticker-level duplicate-lot synchronization; stable row order; unchanged sleeve and sizing formulas; ticker/company autocomplete; automatic quote on selection; ASK-seeded Entry and 5%-below-ASK Stop; one live stop-distance badge; no manual quote button; orange/black theme and green/red/blue financial meanings. This requested readability change supersedes the previous 35px/.70rem table-size requirement. Keep desktop panes adjacent when there is room; do not shrink the font to force narrow panes.
- **Intentionally unchanged:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, v7 route, formulas (`risk_sizing.py`, `trade_math.py`), ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE client/OAuth/session logic, GEX, Holdings, Schwab, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, `streamlit_app.py`, and `terminal_core.py`. Existing unrelated GEX working-copy edits were left untouched in a separate checkout.
- **Tests performed:** Read architecture and prior Risk fix history; traced `streamlit_app.py → v7 → v10 → v9 → v2`; inspected the attached screenshot and authenticated live Risk screen before edits. Syntax-checked the full production import path and new test. Existing Risk intent/checkbox regression passed. New real Streamlit AppTest exercises the production v7 route with synthetic broker responses: duplicate lots check/uncheck and synchronize, sleeve totals/room recalculate, ASK/Stop seed, stop edits change shares, ticker changes auto-quote and reseed, exactly one stop badge/no quote button, defined-risk spread results. At 1280px and 900px panes stack without horizontal overflow; 900px table cells have no clipping. - **Lesson:** Measure rendered font sizes, row stride, wrapper margins and current widget DOM rather than assuming CSS selectors apply. Prefer native container gaps and `st.html` for HTML geometry; enlarge type and recover wasted space before shrinking financial content. Keep responsive rules anchored to the exact Risk workspace, never a broad ancestor `:has()` selector.
