## 2026-09-23 — Persist E*TRADE Risk Book LONG-TERM check marks

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book LONG-TERM intent persistence only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py` and the Risk-owned zero-height browser-state component `src/components/risk_intent_state_v1/index.html`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`.
- **What was broken:** Checking a ticker in the Risk Book `LONG-TERM` column only lived in Streamlit session state. A fresh browser/app session could therefore forget the user's manual LONG-TERM choice even though the ticker was still held.
- **Root cause:** The existing account+ticker intent map was session-only. The duplicate-row-safe checkbox keys and ticker-level override behavior were correct, but there was no durable state layer for those ticker choices.
- **What was changed:** Added a Risk-local browser persistence layer that stores only the checked ticker symbols plus a revision number, scoped by a hashed account token. On render, saved LONG-TERM tickers are restored into the existing ticker-level override map. The saved set is reconciled against the current E*TRADE holdings symbols, so a ticker remains checked until the user unchecks it or the ticker disappears from holdings; when it disappears, its saved LONG-TERM state is removed automatically. Existing checked choices in a live pre-upgrade session are migrated into the new persisted state. The existing native checkbox grid, duplicate-row-safe widget keys, row synchronization, styling, sizing math, and classification formulas were not changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** LONG-TERM intent is ticker-level while rendered checkbox keys remain row-unique, so duplicate lots cannot cause `StreamlitDuplicateElementKey`. A checked ticker stays checked across reruns/page refreshes/app sessions in the same browser until explicitly unchecked or absent from the current holdings set. Never persist balances, positions, OAuth credentials, or the raw E*TRADE account key for this feature; only ticker symbols and a revision are stored, and the account storage namespace uses a hash.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, Part 2 quote/sizing behavior, Holdings tab, GEX, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, navigation, Schwab Risk Sizing, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the prior Risk Book LONG-TERM fix history; traced production route `streamlit_app.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → v9/v2 helpers`; inspected the exact branch diff. Temporary validation run `35924173241` installed production requirements, byte-compiled the full production Risk path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py` (including duplicate SGOL rows, check/uncheck persistence state, account-key hashing, and sold-ticker pruning), rendered all production top-level tabs via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on PR #50 implementation head in Terminal Architecture Guard run `35924173244`; final clean head is revalidated after removing the temporary workflow and appending this entry.
- **Commit SHA:** Risk browser component `9a00a6faf0c0bfb3e44fcb137a85e4fe149fd270`; persistence implementation `fbadb0d846dabaaeb1aaef7003c726ffab4a25c6`; regression coverage `348853ae6e752e57c0d23ebca30352b20d50e41e`.
- **Lesson:** Persist user intent separately from rendered row identity. The durable key is account+ticker intent; row IDs are only for safe Streamlit widget identity. Reconcile persisted intent with the live holdings universe so sold positions clean themselves up without changing portfolio math or unrelated UI.

## 2026-09-23 — Add strict repository-wide agent change-isolation guardrails

- **Feature changed:** Repository coding instructions / development safety only; no terminal runtime feature changed.
- **Exact files changed:** `AGENTS.md` (new), `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (new), `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and this `src/TERMINAL_CHANGELOG.md` entry.
- **What was broken:** Coding agents could follow feature ownership guidance yet still make unsolicited theme, CSS, layout, refactor, formatting, or cross-feature changes when a task did not request them.
- **Root cause:** The repository had strong architecture guidance but no root-level canonical agent contract requiring a pre-edit file whitelist, protected-file treatment, every-line diff justification, and explicit prohibition on unrelated appearance/refactor changes.
- **What changed:** Added root `AGENTS.md` as the mandatory repository-wide coding safety contract. It requires agents to read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and relevant changelog history; trace the production import path; establish an explicit file whitelist before editing; preserve unrelated code/theme; protect shared files; audit every changed line against the request; validate before claiming completion; and append future fixes to this changelog. Added `.github/copilot-instructions.md` so GitHub coding agents are explicitly directed to the same contract. Added the same mandatory pre-edit gate to `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** A feature request authorizes only the smallest owner-file patch needed for that feature. Theme/global CSS/shared infrastructure are not authorized unless explicitly requested or proven unavoidable. Convenience never justifies cross-feature edits.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** All Streamlit runtime code, Risk Sizing behavior/math/UI, GEX, Holdings, OAuth/session logic, navigation, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, `src/theme.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, `.streamlit/config.toml`, and `streamlit_app.py`.
- **Tests / verification:** Read the production architecture and prior changelog before changes; confirmed no prior `AGENTS.md` or `.github/copilot-instructions.md`; branch diff is restricted to instruction/documentation files; no runtime code was modified, so feature syntax/runtime behavior is unchanged. Architecture guard/CI is checked on the PR/head before merge.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on PR #51 instruction head `887c815a8ca64bb659850118d5e9236d9e630f52` in Terminal Architecture Guard run `35929150533`.
- **Commit SHAs:** `AGENTS.md` commit `1cf213ee72fc4d575f185276a20f4e73b1acc291`; GitHub agent instruction commit `3af198952b526cf8c6341111a0ece8b0518ee3b5`; architecture pre-edit gate commit `45af51b3c08a2a82e9a583a95c9e3f1a0a7bb7f5`.
- **Lesson:** Instruction safety must be repository-visible and checked before coding, not only remembered conversationally. Future agents must establish scope before editing and reject their own unrelated diff lines before commit.

## 2026-09-23 — Normalize native dropdown text weight across terminal account/select controls

- **Feature changed:** Shared native Streamlit dropdown typography consistency, including the E*TRADE account selector shown in Holdings and the same selector path used by Risk Sizing and Orders; Option Book's local selectbox override was normalized to the same shared weight.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/theme.py` and `src/option_book_ui.py`. Focused regression coverage added in `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`.
- **What was broken:** The closed/selected value in native Streamlit dropdowns could render visibly thinner than the opened menu options. The supplied Holdings screenshot showed the selected E*TRADE account in a light Courier weight while the dropdown rows were much heavier.
- **Root cause:** The shared terminal dropdown theme styled legacy BaseWeb select triggers and the detached listbox options, but current Streamlit can render the closed trigger through React Aria markup (`aria-haspopup="listbox"` / `role="group"`). The menu options were explicitly weight 800 while that newer trigger path escaped the shared font-weight rule and inherited a lighter weight. Option Book also retained one local BaseWeb select override at weight 900, which could disagree with the shared weight 800.
- **What changed:** Extended only the shared dropdown typography selectors in `src/theme.py` to cover current React Aria select triggers while preserving legacy BaseWeb support. Closed selected values and opened menu options now use the same `Courier New` weight 800. Normalized only Option Book's local selectbox text override from 900 to 800 so it cannot reintroduce a mismatch on legacy/BaseWeb markup. No size, spacing, row height, color, border, account selection behavior, or data logic changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Bloomberg orange/black surfaces and white dropdown rows remain unchanged; selected rows remain orange with black text; native Streamlit select behavior remains intact; E*TRADE account labels/masking/default account/session stickiness remain unchanged. Holdings, Risk Sizing, and Orders continue using the same shared `_account_picker`; Option Book keeps its own account selection logic.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/holdings_snapshot_mode.py`, Risk Sizing production files, `src/terminal_core.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, GEX, OAuth/session plumbing, navigation, formulas, holdings data/classification logic, `.streamlit/config.toml`, and all non-selectbox typography.
- **Tests performed:** Read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and relevant changelog history; traced the production account-selector paths; audited production selectbox CSS for stronger local overrides. GitHub Actions temporary validation run `35932648150` installed production requirements, syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py`, `src/theme.py`, and `src/option_book_ui.py`; passed `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`; passed `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across all top-level routes; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in full validation run `35932648150`; clean PR #52 head `e59a48ed6fe6c711f0bd1d53fa1a23608c29403a` also passed Terminal Architecture Guard run `35932797032`.
- **Commit SHAs:** shared dropdown fix `f5ade4c9b395215b842f4b867ff4b7b9e8c04c14`; Option Book normalization `be65d88ef59b330b4a10ff658e384e6a7e4d269a`; regression guard `ad0ef4466aad6d125edf6533de0c078652c891c9`.
- **Lesson:** Native Streamlit dropdowns may use different DOM implementations across versions. Shared typography rules must cover both the closed trigger and the detached menu portal, and local feature CSS must not override the shared text weight for the same control.

## 2026-09-23 — Lock E*TRADE account picker typography across label, closed value, and open rows

- **Feature changed:** Shared E*TRADE account-selector typography only, as rendered in Holdings, E*TRADE Risk Sizing, and Orders through the common `E*TRADE Account` selectbox.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/theme.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`.
- **What was broken:** After the prior dropdown-weight fix, the live terminal still showed the closed E*TRADE account value visibly thinner than the opened account rows even after a full app restart.
- **Root cause:** The prior validation checked source selectors rather than the actual rendered control. A real Chromium probe showed Streamlit 1.61+ renders the selected account as the value of a React Aria `<input role="combobox">`, while opened account choices render as `<div role="option">` nodes in a detached `<div role="listbox" aria-label="E*TRADE Account">` portal. Although both computed to Courier New / 14px / weight 800 in the isolated probe, native input text and ordinary div text can rasterize differently across browsers/platforms. The earlier test therefore proved CSS values, not visual element-type consistency.
- **What changed:** Added one E*TRADE-account-specific typography contract in the shared theme that targets the widget label, the React Aria combobox input, and the detached E*TRADE account listbox rows directly. All three now use the same real-bold Courier face at 14px / weight 700 with identical line height, letter spacing, font synthesis, text rendering, and font smoothing rules. Colors, borders, row heights, spacing, option highlight colors, account labels, masking, defaults, and selection behavior were not changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Holdings, Risk Sizing, and Orders continue using the same shared account picker and existing session/default-account behavior. The selected account remains orange on black; unselected opened rows keep their existing colors; the selected opened row keeps its orange background/black text. No custom dropdown component or account-logic fork was introduced.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/holdings_snapshot_mode.py`, Risk Sizing production files, `src/terminal_core.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, Option Book behavior/UI, GEX, OAuth/session plumbing, navigation, formulas, holdings data/classification logic, `.streamlit/config.toml`, and all non-account dropdown controls.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and prior dropdown/theme history; traced the production shared account-picker path. Browser probe run `35933833764` inspected the real Streamlit React Aria DOM and established the selected-value input versus detached option-div structure. Full validation run `35934242797` installed production requirements plus Chromium, rendered a production-theme E*TRADE account selectbox, opened the real listbox, and measured the closed input and opened option at identical `Courier New, Courier, monospace`, 14px, weight 700. The same run passed Python syntax checks, `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`, `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across all top-level routes, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS inside full validation run `35934242797`; clean PR #53 head `abd29c01c0a75f6b37c2d2e6665b2f008fa0d933` passed Terminal Architecture Guard run `35934405736`. Post-merge main is checked before completion.
- **Commit SHAs:** production theme fix `df1a1773fad2c5b6d4eb7676fc59f2ae7d760c28`; regression update `24e9461cccf43368cfbd3553bc3f61259e97470c`.
- **Lesson:** For Streamlit React Aria selects, validate the actual rendered input and detached listbox—not only CSS source. Equal computed weights do not guarantee identical visual rendering across different element types, so visually critical shared controls should receive one explicit typography contract at the real DOM nodes.

## 2026-09-25 — Align CBOE GEX calculations with supplied Google Apps Script

- **Feature changed:** CBOE delayed-options GEX calculation parity for CBOE Overview and the separate CBOE raw TXT / TradingView bridge.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`. Documentation updated in this changelog. No GEX UI/publisher file required a runtime change because the existing CBOE refresh already feeds the same CBOE result map to both Overview and `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`.
- **What was broken:** At the same CBOE SPY snapshot (spot 767.18, 180 DTE), the terminal/raw CBOE path showed CALLWALL 785 and GFLIP about 770.70 while Raj's supplied Google Sheets Code.gs definition selected CALLWALL from max call OI (800) and used a different gamma-flip scan. This made the terminal/raw output disagree with the sheet even though the raw TXT and Streamlit CBOE view agreed with each other.
- **Root cause:** The isolated CBOE engine had intentionally inherited the production E*TRADE wall/flip definitions. Raj's supplied Code.gs uses different CBOE definitions: CALLWALL = max-call-OI strike, PUTWALL = minimum net-GEX strike, and gamma flip = a 1,000-step full-strike-range Black-Scholes rescan using 4.5% risk-free rate, 0.5-day time floor, and the sign crossing nearest current spot.
- **What changed:** Kept the existing CBOE contract filtering, feed gamma preference/fallback, and exposure formula `gamma × OI × 100 × spot² × 1%`. Changed only the CBOE wall selectors and gamma-flip implementation to match the supplied Code.gs. The new flip implementation evaluates the same 1,001 scan prices in bounded NumPy batches so it preserves the Apps Script math without creating an unnecessarily large matrix. CBOE refresh/publish routing remains unchanged and still writes only the source-isolated CBOE bridge.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** CBOE still uses the CBOE delayed option-chain payload, positive CBOE feed gamma when supplied, the same IV/Black-Scholes fallback when gamma is missing, all eligible contracts at or below each ticker's saved DTE, calls as +GEX, puts as −GEX, and the separate `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt` output. A CBOE refresh must update Overview and then republish that same CBOE result map. E*TRADE GEX formulas and bridge semantics remain untouched.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_ui.py` E*TRADE GEX math/IV logic, `src/gex_ui_v3.py` layout/refresh/publisher routing, `src/gex_github_bridge.py`, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and prior CBOE history; traced `streamlit_app.py → src/gex_workspace_v2.py → src/gex_ui_v3.py → src/gex_cboe.py`. Temporary validation run `36093630553` installed production requirements, syntax-checked the CBOE production path, passed 6 focused CBOE tests including exact feed-gamma exposure values, wall-definition divergence cases, and an independent scalar Code.gs gamma-flip reference, fetched the live CBOE SPY chain, and calculated 180-DTE SPY from 5,701 usable contracts at spot 767.18. The corrected live calculation selected CALLWALL 800 (matching max call OI), PUTWALL 760 (the minimum net-GEX strike in that live snapshot), GFLIP 767.891229, and top absolute GEX strike 785. The same run passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` and `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across the production top-level tabs. Separate Terminal Architecture Guard run `36093630532` also passed on the production-code head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on production-code head `ccc05892a3e77d72094c5de25a4ed27ae5cd85ff`; clean PR head is revalidated after removing the temporary workflow and appending this entry.
- **Commit SHAs:** CBOE production calculation fix `4a4a9a0096017cd7b62627ab080f8a3856274a6d`; focused regression coverage `daa054f12ad6868e3cd2b6a54622dcb5ac84afda`.
- **Lesson:** Source parity requires matching both the market-data snapshot and the wall/flip definitions. If Streamlit and its raw bridge agree but an external sheet differs, verify the calculation semantics before changing transport or UI code.
## 2026-09-25 — Add CBOE snapshot fingerprint/time and remove GEX IV Rank column

- **Feature changed:** GEX source-comparison diagnostics and Overview column simplification.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, and `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`.
- **What was broken / confusing:** Streamlit CBOE and `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt` could agree with each other while a separately refreshed Google Sheet showed different wall/flip values from a different CBOE delayed-chain snapshot. The Overview also still carried the IV Rank / Ref column even though Raj no longer wants that column.
- **Root cause:** CBOE requests had no deterministic visible identifier for the exact GEX-driving inputs, so two requests with the same rounded spot could not be proven to contain the same OI/IV/gamma rows. The IV column was injected locally by the production GEX wrapper into both source views.
- **What changed:** Each CBOE result now records a `CBOE1` SHA-256 fingerprint over the exact fields that can change this GEX calculation: spot plus each option OCC id, open interest, IV, and feed gamma. The digest is stable to row ordering and intentionally ignores bid/ask/volume because those fields do not feed GEX. Each result also records its CBOE fetch timestamp and raw option-row count. CBOE Overview keeps the existing UPDATED column but now adds a compact 12-character fingerprint beneath the time; hovering exposes the full fingerprint, exact fetch timestamp, and raw row count. The separate CBOE raw TXT/TradingView block now includes the exact timestamp, full fingerprint, and raw option-row count before the packed gamma rows. The IV Rank / Ref presentation column and its local tooltip/CSS injection were removed from both E*TRADE and CBOE Overview tables. Existing IV calculations/history were not changed because the request was to remove the column, not alter E*TRADE GEX math.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** CBOE Code.gs-parity wall/flip/GEX formulas from the immediately prior fix remain unchanged. The fingerprint must change when spot, OI, IV, feed gamma, or option identity changes, but not merely when bid/ask/volume changes or row order changes. CBOE raw TXT remains separate from E*TRADE `latest_gex.txt`, and Pine continues ignoring non-packed metadata lines. Keep the Overview compact; snapshot diagnostics belong inside the existing UPDATED cell rather than adding another wide column.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_ui.py` E*TRADE GEX/IV formulas, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, `src/gex_github_bridge.py`, OAuth/session plumbing, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme, authentication, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`. The uploaded Perfiliev gamma-profile article/PDF was reviewed separately but its profile/chart methodology was not merged into production in this change.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and prior CBOE/GEX history; traced `streamlit_app.py → src/gex_workspace_v2.py → src/gex_ui_v3.py → src/gex_cboe.py`. Temporary validation run `36095447631` installed production requirements, syntax-checked all affected Python files, passed 8 focused CBOE tests, verified fingerprint stability/sensitivity rules, verified the IV Rank / Ref header is absent from production GEX UI source, fetched live CBOE SPY with 13,028 option rows, and calculated a live snapshot fingerprint `CBOE1:9641DEE9A39F…` at 2026-09-25T00:41:54-04:00 with 5,701 usable <=180-DTE contracts. The same run passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` and `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across all eight production top-level tabs plus repeated GEX/Risk smoke routes. Separate Terminal Architecture Guard run `36095447585` passed on the production-code head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation head `965e4df9733875a7b79df8988899403bc0d8fd3d`; clean PR head is revalidated after removing the temporary workflow and appending this entry.
- **Commit SHAs:** snapshot engine metadata `9741783a1b53a8ce97d91f4f6c6a21bc42e2c665`; raw TXT serializer `03a20f7ffcb1b2c097666f5005958c5ef669dd26`; Overview cleanup/snapshot display `be409eae8633dddeed51cef9e76ab416a31b0ae4`; focused regression coverage `a13b8dd6eb86d9615c972dd029d0eae09cbfccad`.
- **Lesson:** When comparing independently refreshed market-data consumers, a timestamp alone is insufficient. Persist and display a deterministic hash of the exact calculation inputs so equal/different snapshots can be proven rather than inferred from rounded spot prices.


## 2026-09-25 — Make E*TRADE Risk Sizing reactive and add E*TRADE-first Yahoo quote fallback

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing interaction performance, Part 2 layout containment, ticker autocomplete performance, and quote-source fallback.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, and `src/ticker_autocomplete.py`; dependency declaration in `requirements.txt`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken / slow:** Risk widget changes were normal Streamlit app reruns, so a stop/size/ticker interaction could re-execute unrelated top-level terminal work. The ticker directory could block cold startup on two serial Nasdaq Trader HTTP calls and the 10k+ selector labels were rebuilt during otherwise unrelated Risk edits. The hidden legacy quote-button column also changed its horizontal row to block layout, while Part 2 columns/cards had no explicit min-width containment, leaving avoidable overlap risk. Quote loading had no current-market fallback beyond E*TRADE/cache behavior when the live E*TRADE quote path was unavailable or unusable.
- **Root cause:** Risk Sizing was not a Streamlit fragment; autocomplete mixed network refresh and label construction into the interactive render path; Part 2 depended on a CSS block-layout workaround for its hidden second column; and quote source selection was a single E*TRADE path whose shared cache may intentionally return a stale E*TRADE snapshot after a failed live request.
- **What changed:** The production v10 Risk renderer is now a Streamlit fragment, so Risk interactions rerun the Risk workspace rather than the full terminal script. Its temporary Streamlit/header wrappers remain scoped to that fragment render and are restored in `finally`. Part 2 keeps the exact existing orange/black visual contract while the ticker row remains flex-based, the unused quote-button column stays hidden, and Risk-only columns/cards are allowed to shrink within their own grid without overlapping siblings. Ticker directory loading now serves an existing full disk snapshot immediately and refreshes it in a daemon background thread; first-ever Nasdaq directory downloads run concurrently instead of serially. The large `SYMBOL — COMPANY NAME` label set is cached against the current symbol/history snapshot so stop/size edits do not rebuild it unnecessarily. Quote loading now forces a live E*TRADE quote first when a live client exists. If that request fails, transparently falls back to a stale E*TRADE snapshot, returns no usable ask, or E*TRADE is offline, Risk requests a 15-second-cached `yfinance` quote. If Yahoo also fails, the previous last-known E*TRADE snapshot remains the final fail-safe. Entry and Stop continue seeding from ASK; when Yahoo does not supply an ask, its latest usable price is explicitly treated as the fallback sizing seed and recorded as such in quote metadata/tooltips.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Live E*TRADE is always first priority for ticker quotes. Keep ticker/company autocomplete, automatic quote loading on ticker selection, Entry seeded from ASK, Stop seeded 5% below the sizing seed, exactly one live stop-distance indicator, no manual quote button, current Risk formulas, current Risk Book LONG-TERM behavior, and the established Bloomberg orange/black styling. The Yahoo path is only a quote fallback; it must never replace E*TRADE holdings/account data or alter sizing formulas.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/theme.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Option Book, Schwab Risk Sizing, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and all shared terminal appearance files.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and the prior Risk history; traced `streamlit_app.py → risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → v9/v2`. Temporary GitHub Actions validation run `36156891178` installed production requirements including yfinance 1.7.0; syntax-checked the full Risk production path, ticker autocomplete, and `streamlit_app.py`; passed the production Risk AppTest covering the existing duplicate-lot/sleeve math, ASK/Stop seed, ticker auto-quote, stop edits, spread sizing, the v10 fragment contract, E*TRADE-first failure ordering, Yahoo fallback source, and fallback Entry/Stop seed; passed the LONG-TERM intent regression; rendered every production top-level tab plus repeated GEX/Risk routes; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in temporary validation run `36156891178` on implementation/test head `69e40cd7c2ebce08d26f9eb42ed5a4fefbc44caa`. The clean PR head is revalidated after this changelog entry and temporary-workflow removal.
- **Commit SHAs:** reactive/fallback v10 implementation `192ade793b000ec9a2dc4eb90c87e0554afc095b`; quote-source preservation `7d07afcdac6ecbc9f46bea76620c9123ebcf3fd5`; autocomplete performance `7de7062c97a4a0cd674084e4b40770a9e4bc5d49`; yfinance dependency `0f5b439a812f4362d9701f6513715054c71131ba`; focused regression coverage `bdcaeac9355daaec498bb408796ed296221ca7a9` and `69e40cd7c2ebce08d26f9eb42ed5a4fefbc44caa`.
- **Lesson:** Keep market-data fallback ordering explicit and source-aware: live broker first, independent current-data fallback second, stale broker snapshot last. For interactive Streamlit workspaces, move expensive feature work into fragments and keep network/directory refreshes off the hot widget-rerun path instead of compensating with broader page reruns or global Streamlit patches.


## 2026-09-25 — Complete disconnected E*TRADE ticker fallback and live-source retry

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing ticker quote fallback when no E*TRADE client exists, plus recovery back to the live broker source after a temporary fallback.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was still missing:** The first reactive/yfinance fix handled live E*TRADE quote failures and the normal offline-snapshot client, but the base Risk renderer exits before Part 2 when there is no E*TRADE client at all. That meant a first-use/full-disconnect session could not reach the Yahoo ticker fallback. Also, after a successful Yahoo fallback for a live-but-failing broker client, the same ticker could remain on the fallback source indefinitely because the quote was already cached in session state.
- **Root cause:** Quote fallback was correctly implemented inside the Part 2 ticker hook, but a completely absent E*TRADE client short-circuits before that hook is rendered. The quote reload predicate also treated any populated same-symbol quote as final without considering whether the source was the preferred live broker source.
- **What changed:** v10 now renders the existing ticker-search/quote-card language in a disconnected quote-only mode when no E*TRADE client exists. It uses Yahoo Finance without invoking E*TRADE account callbacks and clearly states that account-based risk budgets/position sizing require E*TRADE portfolio context. When a live E*TRADE client does exist, a Yahoo/cached fallback is retried against live E*TRADE after a 15-second retry window on the next Risk interaction; immediate edits within that window reuse the fallback and avoid a request storm. No sizing formulas or account data were moved to Yahoo.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Live E*TRADE remains the preferred quote source whenever a live client exists. Yahoo is quote-only fallback. E*TRADE/cached brokerage data remains authoritative for holdings, balances, sleeve math, and risk budgets. The disconnected view must not fabricate account sizing data. The current orange/black theme, ticker/company autocomplete, automatic quote loading, ASK/5%-below-ASK seeding when sizing context exists, and compact layout remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/ticker_autocomplete.py`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/theme.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, GEX, navigation, Schwab Risk, Option Book, Bull Debit, Muni, and Orders.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions validation run `36157687094` installed production requirements; syntax-checked v7/v10/v9/v2, ticker autocomplete, the focused Risk test, and `streamlit_app.py`; passed the production Risk AppTest covering the existing sizing workflow, E*TRADE-first failure ordering, immediate fallback reuse, a true `client=None` Yahoo quote-only path that proves account callbacks are not invoked, and disconnected quote seeding; passed the LONG-TERM intent regression; rendered every production top-level tab plus repeated routes; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in temporary validation run `36157687094` on test head `6a46269446144de636b2c91ea9991864d1fc9882`. The clean PR head is revalidated after temporary-workflow removal.
- **Commit SHAs:** disconnected/retry implementation `09457bcd092c2dd7250db5930102e67a38fb0e22`; focused regression updates `861605a5c5503e2671d565f4d51a30d309b8c476` and `6a46269446144de636b2c91ea9991864d1fc9882`.
- **Lesson:** A fallback inside a downstream widget is not sufficient if an upstream renderer can exit before the widget exists. Keep degraded quote research reachable without inventing portfolio context, and give preferred-source recovery a short retry interval so fallback data does not become accidentally sticky.


## 2026-09-25 — Persist E*TRADE Risk Book across browser sessions

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing last-known portfolio memory and degraded-mode continuity.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, and new Risk-only browser component `src/components/risk_book_state_v1/index.html`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was missing:** The shared E*TRADE offline vault already kept last-known holdings/balances in Streamlit server memory, but that cache intentionally disappears on a process restart/redeploy. As a result, opening Risk Sizing while E*TRADE was disconnected, still loading, or unable to return account/portfolio data could leave the Risk Book unavailable even though the same browser had successfully loaded it in the prior session.
- **Root cause:** Risk Sizing had no durable feature-local snapshot outside Streamlit process memory. The existing browser persistence covered only LONG-TERM intent checkboxes, not the actual Risk Book holdings/account totals or Risk control values.
- **What changed:** Added a zero-height Risk-only browser-memory component backed by this browser's `localStorage`. After a successful Risk render, the app stores only the fields needed to rebuild the Risk Book and its calculations: symbol, security type/CUSIP, market value, gain/loss dollars and percent, last account total/cash values, the selected account key/name, portfolio snapshot timestamp, and current Risk controls. OAuth tokens, consumer secrets, passwords, option chains, and other terminal data are not stored in this browser snapshot. On a later session/redeploy, v10 checks for that persisted Risk Book before falling back to the quote-only disconnected view. The v2 renderer uses the remembered book when E*TRADE is absent, the account list is still unavailable, the same account's portfolio request fails, or the same account's balance request fails. A compact `RISK BOOK MEMORY` caption identifies stale remembered data and its age. When live E*TRADE data becomes available, it replaces the remembered snapshot and is persisted for the next session. A confirmed live empty portfolio clears the old remembered rows instead of resurrecting sold positions. Existing LONG-TERM intent browser persistence continues unchanged.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Live E*TRADE remains authoritative whenever it successfully returns the selected account's data. Never substitute a remembered snapshot from a different selected account. Cached Risk Book data may be used only as last-known E*TRADE context and must be visibly identified as remembered/stale. Keep all Risk formulas, LONG-TERM behavior, E*TRADE-first quote logic, Yahoo quote fallback, current orange/black presentation, and the fragment/reactive behavior unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_data_cache.py` shared cache behavior, `src/session_persistence.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/login code, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, ticker autocomplete, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk Sizing, Option Book, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, shared theme/config, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-read `AGENTS.md`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and Risk/offline-cache history; traced `streamlit_app.py → risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → v9/v2`. Temporary GitHub Actions runs `36164965056` and final implementation revalidation `36165203390` installed production requirements; syntax-checked the full Risk production path and `streamlit_app.py`; passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including live snapshot creation, a new last-session `client=None` Risk Book restore with E*TRADE account callbacks prohibited, restored Risk control values, and continued Yahoo quote fallback; passed the LONG-TERM intent regression; rendered every production top-level tab plus repeated GEX/Risk routes; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in final temporary validation run `36165203390` on implementation/test head `5a0bda3d6ced77350b99536cca38642decae9631`. The clean PR head is revalidated after removing the temporary workflow and appending this entry.
- **Commit SHAs:** browser-memory component `65eefd557673061353b7a7f7c862b370b78ed296`; snapshot helpers `d3cda0b103d6fa3f1a0cf9ad6c3b13e42a440792`; live/fallback Risk Book loading `a11526cf1debe7c17db8c2efdbeb28ac769c5716`; end-of-render persistence `4d5d3826970e4b2068e4015deff2c5360ec8086e`; v10 persisted-book routing `f556f5cdc9c33f4e7cc031ec1105a4e325a22af2`; focused regression `a4e41dd909dc2b3988b8188eb932e406f7038d9f`.
- **Lesson:** Process-memory brokerage caches are useful for short outages but are not sufficient for "last session" continuity. Persist the minimum feature-specific snapshot in the user's browser, keep credentials out of it, label stale financial data clearly, and replace it only with confirmed live data from the same account.


## 2026-09-25 — Guard E*TRADE cache import during Streamlit Cloud hot reload

- **Feature changed:** Terminal startup/import resilience during Streamlit Cloud GitHub code pulls.
- **Exact production file changed:** `streamlit_app.py`; focused regression added in `scripts/test_streamlit_hot_reload_import_guard.py`.
- **What was broken:** During a live Streamlit Cloud code pull, startup failed at `from src.etrade_data_cache import ...` with `KeyError: 'src.etrade_data_cache'` inside Python's import machinery. The module exists on `main` and imports normally, including on Python 3.14, so this was not a missing file or dependency error.
- **Root cause:** Streamlit's file-watcher/hot-reload path has a known race where a module can be removed from `sys.modules` while Python is finishing that same import, producing the exact deep-import `KeyError` seen in the deployment log. Python's importer expects the module key to remain present after `exec_module`; a concurrent deletion causes the final `sys.modules.pop(spec.name)` to fail.
- **What changed:** Replaced only the direct `src.etrade_data_cache` startup import with a tiny entrypoint-local helper that retries that exact module-name `KeyError` up to two times after the first attempt, invalidating import caches and using 50ms/100ms backoff. Any different `KeyError`, syntax/import error, or third failure is re-raised normally. The same cache classes/functions are then bound exactly as before.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** This guard is only for the transient hot-reload import race. Do not swallow real import errors, change E*TRADE cache semantics, alter OAuth/session behavior, or add broad exception handling around unrelated module imports.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_data_cache.py`, Risk Sizing, Risk formulas, E*TRADE OAuth/session modules, Holdings, GEX, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, `src/terminal_core.py`, and all feature UI/layout code.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36166291096` used Python 3.14, installed the production requirements, directly imported `src.etrade_data_cache`, syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py` and the cache module, passed the focused retry regression by simulating two exact module-name `KeyError` races plus a non-target `KeyError` that must propagate, rendered every production top-level tab, and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in validation run `36166291096` on implementation head `1c0feb74d3554ce97c67ed9c59d2286405079b9c`. The clean PR head is revalidated after temporary-workflow removal.
- **Commit SHAs:** startup guard `47902a77273110405cb9906bead10bf3c639cdc2`; focused regression `50cd0ac9956f3182771597a00dec36fc9c0ae976`.
- **Lesson:** A deep-import `KeyError` during a Streamlit code pull can be a hot-reload race rather than a missing module. Retry only the exact transient module-name failure at the entrypoint; do not mask real application import errors.

## 2026-09-25 — Remove empty vertical rows from E*TRADE Risk Sizing

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing vertical spacing only.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, and `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** The live Risk screen had two large black dead-space bands: one between the shared `CROWN MACRO RISK ENGINE` subtitle and `HOW TO USE RISK SIZING`, and another between `REFRESH RISK PORTFOLIO` and `1. CLASSIFY THE CURRENT BOOK`.
- **Root cause:** The first gap was not intentional layout padding. The v10/v9/v2 render chain emitted CSS-only `<style>` payloads through `st.markdown(..., unsafe_allow_html=True)`; because v9 reapplies its CSS after the v2 CSS, multiple invisible Markdown elements were inserted into Streamlit's vertical flex stack before the first visible Risk control. The second gap came from the browser-persistence reader/writer custom components for Risk Book/LONG-TERM state. Their iframes correctly report 0px height, but their Streamlit element rows still participated in the parent vertical gap calculation.
- **What changed:** CSS-only Risk styles now use `st.html`, which Streamlit treats as style/event content instead of visible Markdown rows. The four browser-state component calls (Risk Book reader/writer and LONG-TERM intent reader/writer) are wrapped in Risk-owned keyed zero-height shells, and scoped CSS removes those shell element containers from normal document flow while leaving the components mounted and functional. No visible control height, section-bar size, account selector, refresh button, Risk Book row, Part 2 layout, color, typography, formula, or broker behavior was changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep the Risk page compact with no unexplained vertical dead space. Browser persistence must continue to run for last-session Risk Book memory and LONG-TERM ticker intent. Live E*TRADE remains authoritative; Yahoo remains quote fallback only. Keep the direct ticker input + separate Company / ETF display, fragment/reactive updates, current orange/black theme, and all existing sizing math.

## 2026-09-25 — Cap Risk Sizing shares by liquid balance and/or Tactical Room

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 stock/ETF capital-cap controls and stock sizing math.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** Stop-based dollar-risk sizing could recommend a valid risk-budget share count whose position notional required far more deployable cash than the account had available. The existing Tactical Room check only warned after sizing; it did not provide a user-controlled hard capital cap.
- **Root cause:** `stock_position_size` constrained shares only by Max Dollar Risk divided by stop distance. Position notional and available capital were displayed/checked separately, so neither liquid cash nor Tactical Room could reduce MAX SHARES.
- **What changed:** Added an editable `Liquid Balance` field in Part 2, seeded from the existing E*TRADE true-cash value when available. Added native `USE LIQUID BALANCE` and `USE TACTICAL ROOM` switches; active switches use the terminal's existing orange primary color without changing theme/CSS. Both switches default OFF so existing sizing behavior is preserved until the user enables a capital constraint. Liquid Balance is manually editable; Tactical Room is pulled live from the existing Risk summary and updates with Risk Book/sleeve changes. If one source is enabled, its nonnegative dollar amount caps stock/ETF notional. If both are enabled, the lower dollar amount wins. Final shares are `min(floor(Max Dollar Risk / Risk per Share), floor(Active Capital Limit / Entry Price))`. Negative Tactical Room is treated as zero available tactical capital. MAX SHARES, POSITION NOTIONAL, ACTUAL STOP RISK, and UNUSED RISK recalculate immediately from the constrained share count. The existing Tactical Room warning remains when that switch is OFF and notional exceeds room. The new liquid value and switch states persist through the existing Risk Book browser snapshot.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The capital cap is optional and stock/ETF-only. Max Dollar Risk remains an independent loss constraint; enabling a capital switch can only reduce shares, never increase the risk-based size. When both capital switches are enabled, use the smaller capital limit. Defined-risk option spread sizing is unchanged. Preserve direct ticker entry, separate company display, automatic E*TRADE-first quote loading with Yahoo fallback, ASK-seeded Entry, 5%-below-ASK Stop, one live stop-distance badge, native orange/black theme, compact Part 2 layout, and existing Risk Book/LONG-TERM persistence.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, ticker autocomplete/data, E*TRADE OAuth/session/cache plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary PR validation run `36177513646` installed production requirements, syntax-checked `src/risk_sizing.py`, the complete production Risk UI path, `streamlit_app.py`, and the focused regression; passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including liquid-only and Tactical-Room-only hard-cap scenarios plus the supplied SPY-style numeric case; passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; rendered every production top-level tab via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. The normal Terminal Architecture Guard also passed in run `36177513624`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** sizing formula `b622b5d5ae8801e11acaf735f031301834f86b6f`; Part 2 controls `f0ab6a790b14f19b809cf51fe0399910964b9c60`; focused regression final head before temporary-workflow removal `e94f8a8ecee40f9adc6d0ff8cf1387a1fe820f7b`.
- **Lesson:** Stop-loss risk and deployable capital are separate constraints. Keep the Crown risk budget intact, then optionally cap stock/ETF shares by user-selected capital sources; never rely on a post-calculation warning when the user explicitly wants a hard notional limit.

## 2026-09-25 — Auto-sync Risk Liquid Balance from E*TRADE cash, never margin buying power

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Liquid Balance data source and synchronization only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken / confusing:** Liquid Balance was seeded from E*TRADE cash on first use but then persisted as a Risk Book setting, so an older cached/manual value could remain even after the broker cash balance changed. The existing Risk-specific cash selector also prioritized `cashBalance` / `netCash` and did not explicitly prefer E*TRADE's `cashAvailableForInvestment` field.
- **Root cause:** The UI treated Liquid Balance primarily as a persisted user input instead of a broker-sourced value that should refresh when E*TRADE returns newer cash data. The Risk wrapper also lacked an explicit rule that `marginBuyingPower` is diagnostic only and never eligible as cash.
- **What changed:** Risk now prefers E*TRADE `Computed.cashAvailableForInvestment`, which E*TRADE documents separately from `marginBuyingPower`. An exact zero or negative `cashAvailableForInvestment` is respected rather than falling through to a larger cash-looking field. If that field is absent, the fallback order is `cashBalance`, `netCash`, `cashBuyingPower`, `moneyMktBalance`, then `settledCashForInvestment`. `marginBuyingPower` is recorded only for diagnostics and is never selected. When the selected E*TRADE account, cash source, or broker cash amount changes, the Risk Liquid Balance is automatically refreshed from the broker value. A temporary manual override may survive ordinary reruns while the broker value is unchanged. The existing Risk Book browser snapshot still retains the last-known value so disconnected/failed-balance sessions have a fallback; live E*TRADE cash replaces that fallback when available.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The single two-position capital-source control remains unchanged: left is `USE LIQUID BALANCE ENTERED`, right is `USE TACTICAL ROOM`. Margin buying power must never feed the liquid-cash cap. Preserve all sizing formulas, ticker/quote behavior, ASK/Stop seeding, LONG-TERM persistence, and current theme/layout.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, and Orders.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary PR validation run `36183023704` installed production requirements; syntax-checked the complete production Risk path plus `streamlit_app.py`; passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including a synthetic margin account with `cashAvailableForInvestment=1,234.56`, `cashBalance=9,876.54`, and `marginBuyingPower=50,000`, proving the cash field wins and margin is ignored; verified an exact zero `cashAvailableForInvestment` is respected; verified a stale persisted/manual Liquid Balance is replaced by live broker cash; passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; rendered every production top-level tab via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. The standard Terminal Architecture Guard also passed on the corrected PR head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** cash-selection/auto-sync implementation `61da80025c4fb2fb44d667fe1b75877766ce1938`; Liquid Balance help update `678e6f671a11e40b23acbfda4a3b7ced86b93305`; corrected focused regression `01898402171d1899a2deff26974ab85790bda943`.
- **Lesson:** Broker cash and margin buying power are distinct concepts. For stock capital caps, prefer the broker's explicit cash-available field, never substitute margin buying power, and let live broker cash supersede stale cached UI values while retaining last-known cash only as a fallback.


## 2026-09-25 — Fix Risk Liquid Balance to use actual E*TRADE cash and overwrite stale manual values

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Liquid Balance source/refresh behavior only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** A previously entered Liquid Balance such as $100,000 could remain visible after deployment, and the immediately prior implementation preferred E*TRADE `cashAvailableForInvestment`, which is not the same field as the API's explicitly defined current cash balance.
- **Root cause:** Two issues combined. First, Risk reused the shared `terminal_core` session balance dictionary, which can keep the same balance payload until an explicit refresh even though the broker cache underneath has a 30-second balance TTL. Second, the Risk cash selector treated `cashAvailableForInvestment` as authoritative instead of E*TRADE `Computed.cashBalance`, even though E*TRADE documents `cashBalance` as the current cash balance and separately exposes cash/margin buying-power fields.
- **What changed:** Risk now uses E*TRADE `Computed.cashBalance` first and respects an exact zero or negative cash balance. If `cashBalance` is absent, the only fallbacks are `netCash`, `moneyMktBalance`, and `settledCashForInvestment`. `cashAvailableForInvestment`, `cashBuyingPower`, `dtCashBuyingPower`, `marginBuyingPower`, `dtMarginBuyingPower`, and `marginBalance` are diagnostics only and can never become the liquid-cash cap. Whenever a broker balance payload is available, it overwrites any older manually entered/browser-cached `risk_liquid_balance`. Risk also bypasses only the outer session-level balance dictionary on each Risk render; the shared `CachedETradeClient` still enforces its 30-second balance TTL, so the value refreshes automatically without a live API hit on every widget interaction. The existing REFRESH RISK PORTFOLIO action still forces the shared E*TRADE portfolio/balance refresh path.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The single capital-source control remains unchanged: left is `USE LIQUID BALANCE ENTERED`, right is `USE TACTICAL ROOM`. The visible Liquid Balance field is now broker-authoritative whenever E*TRADE balance data is available. Never use any margin buying-power field as cash. Preserve current stock sizing formulas, ticker/quote behavior, ASK/Stop seeding, LONG-TERM persistence, and terminal theme/layout.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v7.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/etrade_data_cache.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, OAuth/session plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, and Orders.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary PR validation run `36184253136` installed production requirements; syntax-checked the complete production Risk path plus `streamlit_app.py`; passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` using a synthetic margin account where `cashAvailableForInvestment=100,000`, `cashBuyingPower=100,000`, `marginBuyingPower=50,000`, but actual `cashBalance=9,876.54`, proving Risk selects the actual cash field. The regression also proves an exact zero cash balance is respected, `netCash` is used only when `cashBalance` is absent, an old manually entered Liquid Balance is replaced by broker cash, and the Risk wrapper requests refresh through the outer balance cache on every render. The same run passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, rendered every production top-level tab via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. The standard Terminal Architecture Guard also passed on the implementation PR head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** production cash/refresh fix `c315ed1eb1aea5e2b64ef2593737af2cd358d6de`; UI help correction `cb8fecea7344f5a4c88c49e31b23d4217a48bc06`; focused regression `7cf57470f2d8aab404757bcd8918deee737f35a9`.
- **Lesson:** For margin accounts, distinguish actual cash from buying power. Use the broker's explicit current cash-balance field, and do not let a persisted user-entered number survive once live broker cash is available.
