## 2026-09-25 — Fix disappearing/black dropdown hover text terminal-wide

- **Feature changed:** shared terminal dropdown hover/focus readability, including the Risk Book SORT menu shown in the reported screenshot.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/theme.py` for terminal-wide detached dropdown menus and `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py` for the later Risk-specific option styling that was overriding the shared hover contract. Regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`.
- **What was broken:** Hovering a dropdown option could turn its text black while the option background remained dark, making labels such as `SORT: SYMBOL` effectively disappear.
- **Root cause:** Risk v9 emitted an unscoped detached-menu rule that forced `[role="option"]:hover *` to black. Streamlit renders select menus in a portal outside the feature container, so the rule could conflict with the shared terminal dropdown background and affect detached option rows broadly. The shared theme also styled the hover/focus parent but did not explicitly force every nested option text node for all React Aria data-state variants.
- **What changed:** Shared dropdown hover/focus/highlight states now force both the option and all descendants to a dark `#171007` background / light amber `#ffad52` foreground contract for BaseWeb and React Aria interaction states. Risk v9 now uses the same readable hover contract. Only the actually selected orange row keeps black text on orange.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** closed select values remain orange on black, normal open-menu choices remain readable, selected rows remain black on orange, account-picker typography remains unchanged, and Risk/Option Book/GEX/Holdings behavior and calculations are untouched.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `streamlit_app.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, navigation, OAuth/session/client/cache logic, Holdings logic, GEX logic, Option Book behavior, Risk math, LONG-TERM persistence, Risk Book sorting/export behavior, and other feature calculations.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36209414041` installed production requirements; syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py`, `src/theme.py`, and the full production Risk v7 → v10 → v9 → v2 route; passed `scripts/test_dropdown_typography.py`, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`, `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. Standard Terminal Architecture Guard run `36209414072` also passed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** shared hover fix `7502d8243576c24af3aa30f0fa0c35a31fd5fdff`; Risk override correction `c4af0105b13aec9469ceca651b0a08526e687bd8`; regression `b15a1babf0d9e82e69fc0adf9e7ae1027d401169`.
- **Lesson:** Detached Streamlit portal menus are shared DOM. Feature CSS must never force unreadable global option hover colors; shared and feature-level portal selectors must agree on both foreground and background, including descendant nodes and React Aria data states.

## 2026-09-25 — Make Touch ID survive app reboots and force fresh-process deploys after commits

- **Features changed:** terminal access / Touch ID persistence and production commit-reboot behavior.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/passkey_auth.py`, `src/lock_screen_v2.py`, `src/components/lock_keypad_v2/index.html`, new `src/reboot_guard.py`, and the production route in `streamlit_app.py`. Repository policy/ownership was updated in `AGENTS.md` and `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Focused regressions were added in `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py` and `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`.
- **What was broken:** Touch ID enrollment depended on `~/.raj_terminal/touch_id_credential.json`, which is only server/container-local and can disappear on a Streamlit app reboot/redeploy. In addition, the current production lock gate in `streamlit_app.py` was still calling the legacy numeric-only `render_app_lock_screen()` even though the newer WebAuthn/Touch ID implementation existed in `src/lock_screen_v2.py`. Production Git updates could also be served by Streamlit hot reload without proving a fresh Python process.
- **Root cause:** The WebAuthn private key/passkey correctly lived on the Mac, but the server-side public verification record had no reboot-durable store. The newer lock module had never been wired into the current production gate. Streamlit hot reload can reuse the same interpreter after Git pulls.
- **What changed — Touch ID:** The production access gate now directly renders `render_seamless_lock_screen(globals())` and does **not** install the lock module's historical OAuth/title wrappers. After enrollment or successful authentication, the non-secret WebAuthn verification record is HMAC-sealed with a key derived from the stable terminal access-code hash and stored in browser `localStorage`. On a cold server/container restart, the keypad returns that sealed envelope; the server verifies the HMAC, hostname/origin, credential version, platform attachment, and internal transport before rebuilding its runtime credential cache and starting the normal Touch ID challenge. Tampered records, records from another hostname, or records signed under a different access-code hash are rejected and removed from browser memory.
- **Biometric privacy:** Raw fingerprint/Touch ID biometric data and the passkey private key are never stored by Raj's Terminal and never leave macOS. Only the public credential ID/public key metadata needed to verify WebAuthn signatures is remembered.
- **Migration behavior:** If the old server-side credential record still exists on the first run after this update, it is automatically sealed into browser memory. If a prior reboot already erased that old record before this fix reached the browser, Raj must enter the access code and enroll Touch ID one more time; subsequent app reboots/redeploys in that browser retain the sealed credential memory.
- **What changed — reboot behavior:** New `src/reboot_guard.py` records the Git commit at interpreter boot using a process-local `builtins` marker. If Streamlit hot-reloads after the checked-out Git commit changes, the stale interpreter exits so the hosting platform starts a fresh Python process. If Git metadata is unavailable, the guard safely no-ops. `AGENTS.md` now requires a fresh-process reboot/redeploy plus deployed health verification after every production commit and prohibits claiming a reboot that was not verified.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The access-code fallback and lockout policy remain intact. Touch ID remains a WebAuthn platform/discoverable passkey with required user verification. E*TRADE OAuth/session logic remains owned by `src/etrade_connection_ui_v2.py`; the old OAuth/title wrappers in `lock_screen_v2.py` are intentionally not installed by the production route. The GEX bridge remains checked before the terminal lock gate. The reboot guard must contain no trading/broker feature logic.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk sizing formulas/UI, GEX calculations/layout, Holdings logic, Option Book behavior, E*TRADE API/OAuth/session transport, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36210965289` installed production requirements; syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py`, `src/reboot_guard.py`, `src/passkey_auth.py`, and `src/lock_screen_v2.py`; passed `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`; passed `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py`, including simulated loss of the server credential file plus restore from the HMAC-sealed browser envelope and rejection of tampered/wrong-secret/wrong-host records; passed the pre-existing `scripts/test_streamlit_hot_reload_import_guard.py`; rendered all top-level tabs through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. Standard Terminal Architecture Guard run `36210965109` also passed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Implementation commits:** sealed credential memory `3cb560c096c6b9949455aebdb37b20345886c8cb`; lock restore path `7c34c08468d6ca83dc66ec5efa0026d4fa2ed19f` / `edfa2ffce58600a20bf45c048d76ec6b54f65621`; keypad browser persistence `d7cb19d36d74d642eb6927127f47a6824e5bd810` / `513f575ef042c0fac0d5595a33c15737b35259f9`; production Touch ID route `48e880946c1e563948e35ca3cce09bd769ab179d`; reboot guard `3951a0e060f46210527ef5d4fc2a71e2570f5a20`; entrypoint reboot invocation `388c96f8654758f4b4a2d47887f26aaaafed7ac2`; policy `9d1213443f95c7bff676fd86f2d306e765d20d89`; architecture map `02e32cd68a6262861c5ce2c099ee55ec330d38d9`.
- **Lesson:** Never persist a WebAuthn trust anchor only in an ephemeral app container. Keep the private key/biometric on the authenticator, persist only integrity-protected public verification metadata, and make hot-deployed production commits cross a real process boundary before declaring completion.

## 2026-09-25 — Harden reboot-safe Touch ID memory with a server-only seal key

- **Feature changed:** terminal Touch ID/passkey persistence security only.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/passkey_auth.py` and `src/lock_screen_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py`.
- **Why this follow-up was required:** The initial reboot-memory implementation correctly stored only public WebAuthn verification metadata, but its HMAC seal was derived from the terminal access-code hash. A durable browser record should instead be authenticated by a server-only secret that is never sent to the browser.
- **What changed:** Browser Touch ID memory is now sealed with `security.touch_id_memory_secret` when configured. If that dedicated secret is absent, Raj's existing server-side E*TRADE `consumer_secret` is used as the stable fallback. The access-code hash remains only the WebAuthn user-identity seed and is no longer the browser-memory MAC key. If neither server-only secret is available, durable browser sealing safely stays unavailable rather than trusting an unsigned browser record.
- **Security behavior that must remain:** Raw fingerprint/Touch ID biometric data and the WebAuthn private key never enter Raj's Terminal. Browser localStorage contains only the public credential verification record plus a server-authenticated HMAC envelope. A modified record, a different hostname, or a different server-only seal secret must be rejected.
- **Important production behavior:** `streamlit_app.py` now uses the v2 Touch ID lock renderer at the actual production gate. Existing access-code fallback/lockout remains. Reboot memory restores the public verification record after a cold app/container restart and then still requires a normal macOS Touch ID/WebAuthn signature before unlocking.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** E*TRADE OAuth/session/client behavior, Risk Sizing, GEX, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36211604110` PASS: production auth/reboot syntax check, `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py` including cold-server-file loss + sealed restore + tamper/wrong-secret/wrong-host rejection, `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`, existing hot-reload regression, all top-level tab rendering, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS in the focused validation run.
- **Implementation commits:** server-only seal hardening `9113de67af70628925162a612466f66ce765c7fa`; lock secret selection `a54ab1bbff7d3ed2081be87beb64decd2f552c33`; focused regression `c4849ea4a8fd87997872c7aa8856653b06f9db10` / `a0743a59d59f8db97f5786320ea2e699aa7bd0f7`.
- **Lesson:** A browser-persisted public WebAuthn record may survive ephemeral hosting, but its integrity key must remain server-only. Never treat browser storage, a public code hash, a biometric template, or an authenticator private key as interchangeable trust anchors.

## 2026-09-25 — Verify the deployed Streamlit endpoint after every main-branch commit

- **Feature changed:** production deployment/reboot verification only.
- **Exact file changed:** `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`.
- **What changed:** The architecture guard now runs on every push to `main` and verifies `https://terminal8.streamlit.app/_stcore/health` after the commit. This Streamlit deployment returns HTTP 303 at its front door, so 200 and the observed 303 redirect are accepted as a reachable deployment endpoint; other statuses continue retrying/failing.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** `src/reboot_guard.py` remains responsible for forcing a stale hot-reloaded Python interpreter to exit when Git HEAD changes. The workflow verifies that the Streamlit deployment endpoint comes back/replies after the commit; it does not change any trading, authentication, or feature logic.
- **Tests performed:** Terminal Architecture Guard run `36211856287` PASS, including production architecture validation, active workflow YAML validation, and the post-push Streamlit deployment endpoint check.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** `7e2c9ec756d7764ee08bacb875613a181f95caf7`.
- **Lesson:** Streamlit Community Cloud may expose a healthy/private front door as an HTTP 303 redirect, so deployment verification must recognize that expected response instead of requiring a literal 200 body.

## 2026-09-25 — Fix Touch ID memory still disappearing after a real app reboot

- **Feature changed:** terminal Touch ID/passkey reboot persistence only.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/lock_screen_v2.py` and `src/components/lock_keypad_v2/index.html`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py`.
- **What was still broken:** After enrolling Touch ID, a real Streamlit app reboot could still return the lock screen to `SET UP TOUCH ID` instead of restoring the previously enrolled credential.
- **Root cause:** The sealed public WebAuthn verification record was still being persisted/restored by the legacy V1 keypad iframe. The server-side HMAC envelope was correct, but the browser persistence transport was tied to the iframe component rather than a first-party app-page storage bridge.
- **What changed:** Reboot-safe browser persistence now uses a Streamlit V2 component registered in `src/lock_screen_v2.py`. V2 JavaScript executes directly in the app page, writes the sealed public verification record to first-party `window.localStorage`, verifies the write by reading it back, and emits explicit save/restore/missing/error events to Python. The V1 keypad remains responsible only for the visible keypad and WebAuthn Touch ID ceremony. It no longer reads or writes localStorage. While a cold-start restore check is running, the Touch ID button now shows `CHECKING SAVED TOUCH ID...` instead of prematurely showing `SET UP TOUCH ID`.
- **Restore behavior:** On a new server/container session with no local credential file, the V2 bridge checks the same browser storage key. A valid HMAC-sealed record is returned to Python, revalidated against the server-only seal secret and terminal hostname/origin, restored to the runtime cache, and then the normal macOS Touch ID challenge proceeds. Missing, tampered, wrong-host, or storage-error states stay locked and require the access-code fallback.
- **Security behavior that must remain:** Raw fingerprint data and the passkey private key never enter Raj's Terminal. Browser storage contains only the already HMAC-sealed public WebAuthn verification metadata. The server still verifies the seal and origin before trusting the restored record.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Access-code fallback/lockout, platform/discoverable WebAuthn behavior, automatic Touch ID prompt after restore, compact lock UI, E*TRADE OAuth/session logic, and the post-commit fresh-process reboot guard remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/passkey_auth.py` cryptographic verification/sealing logic, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client/cache behavior, Risk Sizing, GEX, Holdings, Option Book, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36212527231` installed production requirements and Selenium; syntax-checked the production Touch ID path; passed `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py`; launched a real headless Chrome browser against a temporary Streamlit app using the exact V2 storage bridge, saved the Touch ID memory record, killed the Streamlit server, restarted the server, and confirmed the same browser restored the record successfully; passed `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`, all top-level tab rendering via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Implementation commits:** first-party V2 storage bridge `95aff05f5336aa38ea754cfc343b144cade437cd`; V1 keypad persistence removal/checking state `05bd3ab0c7949c57babe5ddf8aa1c65b705907f1`; regression updates `ee86fe47254d55b4322e129c534468d805ee2a25` and `3ab05b562011dcff575a7bd6486bb1a544e88b48`.
- **Lesson:** For reboot-durable browser state, do not rely on the legacy iframe component as the persistence owner. Keep WebAuthn UI in the iframe if needed, but store the sealed public credential record through first-party app-page storage and test it by actually restarting the Streamlit server while keeping the browser alive.

## 2026-09-26 — Show target stop for zero unused risk and dynamic overused-risk status

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 stock/ETF risk-status result card only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was missing:** The result cards showed ACTUAL STOP RISK and UNUSED RISK, but they did not tell Raj what stop price would use the full Max Dollar Risk at the current MAX SHARES. The status label also had no explicit OVERUSED RISK state for any future/input condition where modeled stop risk exceeds the selected risk budget.
- **Root cause:** The UI displayed only the nonnegative remainder returned by the sizing result. It did not calculate the stop implied by `Entry - (Max Dollar Risk / current shares)`, and the presentation label was hard-coded to UNUSED RISK.
- **What changed:** Added a Risk-UI helper that compares modeled Actual Stop Risk with Max Dollar Risk and returns UNUSED RISK or OVERUSED RISK, the absolute dollar difference, percentage of the risk budget, and the target stop that would make modeled risk equal the selected Max Dollar Risk at the current MAX SHARES. The live card now reads `UNUSED RISK // TARGET STOP $X.XX` or `OVERUSED RISK // TARGET STOP $X.XX`; overused state uses the existing negative/red metric tone. When MAX SHARES is zero, or full use of the risk budget would require a negative stop, TARGET STOP displays N/A. The hover explanation shows the exact formula and retains the slippage warning.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** MAX SHARES continues to be the minimum of the existing risk-based and selected-capital-source limits. This change does not alter Crown risk budgets, share sizing formulas, capital-source logic, E*TRADE cash selection, ticker/quote behavior, ASK-seeded Entry, 5%-below-ASK Stop, LONG-TERM persistence, or defined-risk option spread sizing.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client/cache plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Re-fetched and inspected the exact committed production file; locally sanity-checked the new helper with an UNUSED case (Entry $200, 10 shares, $225 budget, $100 actual risk → $125 unused, 55.56%, target stop $177.50), an OVERUSED case ($250 actual risk → $25 overused, 11.11%, target stop $177.50), and zero shares → TARGET STOP N/A. Updated `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` with AppTest coverage for the rendered target-stop card plus direct UNUSED/OVERUSED helper cases. GitHub Actions `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36269003394` passed on the implementation commit; that workflow ran `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` and verified the production Streamlit health endpoint after the push. The focused AppTest file itself was not separately executable from the available connector/runtime, so no separate focused-test PASS is claimed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation commit `4d64940e1b626b2c3ff0301b11a8e70f1cafb407`.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `4d64940e1b626b2c3ff0301b11a8e70f1cafb407`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** When capital limits reduce the share count, show the stop price that would consume the remaining modeled dollar-risk budget at those shares. Keep risk-budget status presentation separate from the underlying share-sizing formula, and explicitly distinguish unused from overused risk without changing unrelated sizing behavior.

## 2026-09-26 — Stabilize Risk target stop so it does not chase itself

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 TARGET STOP calculation/display only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** After the TARGET STOP was shown, entering that exact displayed stop could make MAX SHARES fall by one and immediately produce a different TARGET STOP. Raj demonstrated the live case where the card moved from about $704.57 at two shares to $637.80 at one share.
- **Root cause:** The first implementation anchored TARGET STOP to the current final share count, which itself depends on Stop Loss. In addition, the exact two-share target in Raj's case was $704.575, while the two-decimal Stop Loss field displayed/accepted $704.57. That one-half-cent rounding direction made two shares exceed the dollar-risk budget by one cent, so the risk limiter cut MAX SHARES to one and the target recalculated again.
- **What changed:** TARGET STOP is now anchored to the maximum whole shares fundable by the selected Capital Source (`capital_limited_shares`), which does not change merely because Stop Loss changes. For a long stock/ETF, the exact target is still `Entry - (Max Dollar Risk / capital-funded shares)`, but the displayed stop is rounded **upward** to the nearest cent so entering it cannot overuse Max Dollar Risk because of cent rounding. The UNUSED/OVERUSED RISK amount remains based on the currently modeled final shares. Help text now explains that a few cents of unused risk can remain because stock stops are entered in $0.01 increments.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** MAX SHARES still equals the existing minimum of risk-limited shares and capital-limited shares. Capital Source selection, E*TRADE cash handling, Crown risk budgets, ticker/quote behavior, ASK-seeded Entry, the 5%-below-ASK initial Stop, LONG-TERM memory, and defined-risk option sizing are unchanged. TARGET STOP must remain stable when Raj enters the displayed target unless Entry, Max Dollar Risk, or the selected Capital Source itself changes.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client/cache plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Added a focused regression that reproduces Raj's reported numbers: Entry $771.35, Max Dollar Risk $133.55, selected capital $2,078.15, and initial Stop $732.78 produce two capital-funded shares. The exact target is $704.575, so the safe displayed target must be $704.58. Re-entering $704.58 keeps MAX SHARES at two, modeled stop risk at $133.54 (not above the $133.55 budget), and TARGET STOP remains $704.58. The exact committed production file was re-fetched and inspected; the commit diff contains only the Risk owner plus its focused regression. GitHub Actions `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36269930353` passed, including `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` and the production Streamlit health check after push. The repository's full focused AppTest/top-tab suite was not separately executable from the available connector/runtime, so no separate PASS is claimed for those commands.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on implementation commit `e88f9b68dd35d69a7397b848264c43e1f0670851`.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `e88f9b68dd35d69a7397b848264c43e1f0670851`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** A displayed target derived from a value that the target itself changes creates a feedback loop. Anchor Risk TARGET STOP to the selected capital capacity and round long-stop prices in the safe direction at the UI's actual cent precision.

## 2026-09-26 — Add Risk math-check hover, client-side target-stop copy, and preserve fragment updates

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 verification/help interaction and TARGET STOP copy action.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was requested:** Raj wanted a hover-over `?` that independently checks whether the displayed Part 2 calculations are mathematically consistent, Part 2 updates without a whole-page refresh, and a small orange icon beside TARGET STOP that copies the stop value for pasting into the Stop Loss field.
- **Root cause / prior limitation:** The existing `?` explained formulas but did not explicitly reconcile every displayed stock-sizing result into a PASS/FAIL arithmetic check. TARGET STOP had no copy action. Risk already rendered through the production `@st.fragment` route, but the new copy action needed to remain browser-side so it would not create an unnecessary Streamlit rerun.
- **What changed:** Added an independent Part 2 arithmetic reconciliation that checks Risk/Share, risk-limited shares, capital-limited shares, final MAX SHARES, ACTUAL STOP RISK, POSITION NOTIONAL, and the stable TARGET STOP modeled risk. The existing `?` on the UNUSED/OVERUSED RISK + TARGET STOP card now starts with `MATH CHECK: PASS` or `MATH CHECK: FAIL` and shows the exact equations using the current values. Added a compact orange `⧉` button immediately beside the TARGET STOP label; it copies the numeric stop price (without the dollar sign) using browser clipboard APIs, briefly shows `✓` on success, and falls back to the browser's legacy copy path when needed. The copy action is pure client-side JavaScript and does not call Streamlit or rerun the fragment.
- **Seamless update behavior:** The production route remains `streamlit_app.py → risk_sizing_ui_v7.py → risk_sizing_ui_v10.py → v9/v2`, with `risk_sizing_ui_v10.py::render_risk_sizing` still decorated with `@st.fragment`. Entry, Stop Loss, Crown size, capital source, and other Risk widget changes therefore rerun only the Risk fragment rather than the whole terminal page. No `st.rerun` was introduced. The clipboard button itself causes no server rerun.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The stable target-stop rule from the prior fix remains unchanged: TARGET STOP is anchored to `capital_limited_shares` and a long stop is rounded upward to the nearest cent so entering it cannot overuse Max Dollar Risk because of cent rounding. MAX SHARES continues to be `min(risk-limited shares, capital-limited shares)`. The math check is arithmetic verification only and does not claim to eliminate execution/slippage risk.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client/cache plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Production push workflow `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36270834750` passed on the implementation commit, including architecture validation and deployed Streamlit health verification. A temporary non-production validation PR (closed after testing and its branch reset) ran workflow `Temporary Risk Math Copy Validation` run `36270911773`; it installed production requirements, syntax-checked the complete Risk production path plus `streamlit_app.py`, passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` with the new MATH CHECK and copy-button markup assertions, passed `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, rendered all top-level tabs via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `beda0c5236abe5ac8e54a472e6985bdec10f3baa`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** Use the existing Risk tooltip as the verification surface instead of adding another card, keep copy actions browser-side when no server state must change, and preserve fragment-scoped reruns so Part 2 feels live without refreshing the whole terminal.

## 2026-09-26 — Recover Risk frontend after direct-page target-copy JavaScript

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing TARGET STOP copy control frontend isolation.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** After the math-check/copy update, Streamlit Cloud could show the browser-level `Oh no. Error running app.` page even though the server health endpoint still returned success. The user-supplied Cloud log showed only the pre-existing `risk_gain_threshold` widget warning and no fatal Python traceback.
- **Root cause:** The copy button had been implemented with JavaScript executed directly in the main Streamlit document through `st.html(..., unsafe_allow_javascript=True)`. That frontend execution path can fail independently of the Python server and is not covered by a simple `/_stcore/health` check. The visible `risk_gain_threshold` line is a Streamlit warning about a widget default plus Session State value; it was not the fatal error.
- **What changed:** Removed the TARGET STOP copy script, button markup, and copy-specific CSS from the shared v9 card HTML, restoring that renderer to its prior stable HTML-only card implementation. The TARGET STOP copy control now renders as a tiny 24x24 isolated `components.html` iframe beside the TARGET STOP card. Clipboard JavaScript executes only inside that iframe, has no access need to the Streamlit main document, causes no server/fragment rerun, and briefly changes the icon from `⧉` to `✓` after a successful copy. The MATH CHECK hover remains unchanged and the Risk production route remains `@st.fragment`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Never run TARGET STOP clipboard JavaScript in the main Streamlit document. Keep the copy control isolated in a tiny iframe. Preserve the arithmetic PASS/FAIL tooltip, the stable capital-funded target-stop anchor, safe cent rounding, fragment-scoped Risk updates, and all existing Risk calculations/quote/capital-source behavior.

## 2026-09-26 — Prevent long-stock Stop Loss from reaching or exceeding Entry Price

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 long STOCK / ETF Stop Loss safety only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** The Stop Loss field could be entered above Entry Price. Because the existing stock sizing math measures absolute Entry-to-Stop distance, an above-entry value could still produce a numerical Risk / Share and MAX SHARES result even though that direction is invalid for the long STOCK / ETF workflow.
- **Root cause:** The Stop Loss number input had a minimum but no dynamic maximum or directional validation against Entry Price. The risk engine correctly remained formula-agnostic, so the long-only UI needed to reject the invalid input before calculations.
- **What changed:** The long-stock Stop Loss control is now hard-capped at least $0.01 below the current Entry Price. Entry and Stop edits both run a Risk-local safety callback that repairs any persisted/out-of-range stop at or above Entry to the nearest valid cent below Entry and marks the correction for a visible warning. A final server-side guard displays `STOP SAFETY // INVALID STOP` and blocks stock sizing if an invalid stop somehow bypasses the widget limit. The safety is implemented entirely in the existing Risk Part 2 UI owner.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Entry/quote seeding, the normal 5%-below-ASK initial Stop, stable TARGET STOP logic and safe cent rounding, MAX SHARES formulas, Crown risk budgets, Capital Source selection, E*TRADE cash handling, ticker/quote behavior, LONG-TERM persistence, Risk Book behavior, fragment-scoped updates, and defined-risk option-spread sizing are unchanged. No global Streamlit patch was added.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client/cache plumbing, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme/config, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary isolated validation run `36276839718` installed production requirements; syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py` plus the complete v7 → v10 → v9 → v2 Risk production path; passed `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including the attempted above-entry Stop regression; passed `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`; and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. PR `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36276835525` passed. After the production merge, push run `36276926857` passed architecture validation, workflow validation, and the production Streamlit health/reboot check.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `ff8371057747775191fb2914cc2d19d68a476634`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** Enforce directional trade-input constraints at the owning UI boundary and again before calculations. Absolute-distance risk math must never make an invalid long stop at/above Entry appear acceptable.

## 2026-09-26 — Stop Community Cloud reboot/update from surfacing the Oh no page

- **Feature changed:** production commit/reboot lifecycle and post-deploy verification only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `streamlit_app.py`, `src/reboot_guard.py`, `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`. Repository guardrail documentation was updated in `AGENTS.md` and `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **What was broken:** Raj's Terminal could show Streamlit Community Cloud's browser-level `Oh no. Error running app.` page whenever a new Git commit was pulled/reloaded, even when the normal health endpoint later reported success.
- **Root cause:** The custom commit-change guard deliberately called `os._exit(75)` when the checked-out Git SHA changed inside an already-running interpreter. On Community Cloud, that kills the managed Streamlit server process from inside the app. That is not Community Cloud's managed reboot path and can be presented to the browser as an app failure.
- **What changed:** Community Cloud is now detected by its managed `/mount/src/...` repository path. `streamlit_app.py` skips the hard-kill guard there, and `src/reboot_guard.py` independently treats Community Cloud as managed lifecycle: on a commit change it updates the boot marker without calling `os._exit`. The existing hard stale-process guard remains for non-Community-Cloud environments. The production workflow now verifies not only `/_stcore/health` but also launches a real headless Chrome session against `https://terminal8.streamlit.app/` and fails if the rendered page contains Streamlit's `Oh no. Error running app` state.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Do not reintroduce an in-app `os._exit` reboot on Streamlit Community Cloud. Community Cloud owns deploy/reboot lifecycle. Keep the non-managed stale-process guard for local/other hosts. Every production push must continue to verify architecture, workflow validity, endpoint health, and the actual rendered frontend.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk Sizing formulas/UI, GEX, Holdings, Option Book, OAuth/session/client/cache logic, Touch ID/WebAuthn behavior, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** PR `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36279468086` passed. Temporary isolated run `36279484101` installed production requirements; syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py`, `src/reboot_guard.py`, and `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`; passed `scripts/test_reboot_guard.py`; passed all top-level tab rendering via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`; passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`; and passed workflow YAML validation. After the production merge, push run `36279530541` passed architecture validation, workflow validation, the production health check, and a real headless-Chrome render of the deployed app with `Raj's Terminal browser render PASS` and no `Oh no` page.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** implementation `575569c4d1ccd1a77a386e8424c7471de47355ce`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** Never use an in-app hard process kill as a substitute for a managed cloud reboot. A successful health endpoint is not enough; verify the actual browser render after every production push.

## 2026-09-26 — Make Risk stop-distance percentage editable

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 2 stop-distance interaction only.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`.
- **What was broken / missing:** The red `▼ 5.00% BELOW ENTRY` stop-distance display was read-only, so changing the percentage required manually translating the desired percentage into a dollar Stop Loss.
- **Root cause:** Risk already derived the percentage from Entry and Stop, but rendered it only as HTML text. There was no native percentage widget or callback to translate an edited percentage back into a cent-valid Stop Loss.
- **What changed:** Replaced the read-only stop-distance badge with one red native number input labeled `▼ % BELOW ENTRY`. It derives from the current Entry/Stop and therefore starts at 5.00% from the existing quote seed. The field accepts direct edits, native +/− controls in 0.25 percentage-point steps, and the existing focused mouse-wheel behavior. Editing the percentage recalculates Stop Loss; editing Stop Loss, Entry, or receiving a new quote recalculates the percentage. The percentage-to-stop conversion remains cent-valid and respects the existing long-stop maximum below Entry. Updates continue through the production `@st.fragment` Risk route with no explicit full-page rerun.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Preserve the normal 5%-below-ASK initial Stop, long-stock Stop Loss safety below Entry, stable TARGET STOP cent rounding, MAX SHARES/risk-budget math, Capital Source behavior, E*TRADE/Yahoo quote fallback, cash handling, Risk Book behavior, and fragment-scoped live updates. Stop Loss remains the persisted source of truth; the percentage is derived from it on render.

Production push run `36280514847` passed architecture/workflow validation, the deployed Streamlit health check, and the real headless-browser `Oh no` check.

## 2026-09-26 — Add reviewed live E*TRADE entry and post-fill protective stop to Risk Sizing

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing live stock-order workflow only.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`; ownership documentation updated in `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`; architecture coverage updated in `scripts/validate_architecture.py`; focused simulated coverage added in `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py` and wired into `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`.
- **What was missing:** Risk Sizing could calculate Entry, Stop, and MAX SHARES but could not submit the reviewed stock entry to E*TRADE or create a protective stop afterward. Raj also needed account selection with his account ending 5474 as the default.
- **Root cause:** The existing production account picker already supported switching accounts and already defaulted to account suffix 5474, but `ETradeClient` intentionally exposed Preview Order only and the Risk UI had no live Place Order / fill-status workflow.
- **What changed:** Kept the existing account picker and 5474 default untouched. Risk Sizing now recomputes the same current stock/ETF MAX SHARES context from the existing risk/capital rules, sends an E*TRADE Preview Order for a DAY BUY LIMIT, presents an explicit final confirmation, and only then permits a live Place Order. The returned E*TRADE order ID is retained in session so the user can check its fill status from E*TRADE Orders. Only a confirmed full fill unlocks the next step; partial fills remain blocked and must be managed in E*TRADE. After a full fill, Risk Sizing previews and separately confirms a GTC SELL STOP at the planned Stop Loss before placing it.
- **Broker/API safety:** The workflow does not pretend E*TRADE supports an automatically linked bracket through this public API path. Entry and protective stop are two separate broker orders. Preview IDs are treated as short-lived and a fresh preview is required before placement. If a Place Order call has an ambiguous/failed response, the terminal marks the submission status uncertain and blocks automatic retries until the user explicitly confirms they checked E*TRADE Orders, preventing accidental duplicate buys/stops.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Account ending 5474 remains the existing default while manual account changes remain sticky for the current session. BUY entry remains LIMIT/DAY using the Risk Entry price and current MAX SHARES. The protective SELL STOP remains locked until the complete entry fill is confirmed, then uses the original planned Stop and GTC duration. Option Book remains preview-only and must not call live `place_order`. Risk's production wrapper must restore its temporary `_render_next_trade` hook in `finally`; do not introduce module-level Streamlit monkey patches.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/terminal_core.py` account-picker/session plumbing, Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` and `src/trade_math.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/login UI, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book UI/helpers, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, and unrelated top-level feature owners.
- **Tests performed:** PR #76 `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36283314570` passed production parsing/architecture validation, active workflow validation, and the dependency-free simulated order test. The simulated test verified the exact production helpers build a DAY equity BUY LIMIT preview, carry the broker preview ID into Place Order, build a GTC SELL STOP, unlock on a full EXECUTED fill, block on an INDIVIDUAL_FILLS partial fill, and never instantiate an E*TRADE client or submit a real trade. Production push run `36283340916` passed architecture validation, the simulated order test, workflow validation, the deployed Streamlit health check, and the real headless-browser check with no `Oh no` page. Temporary validation PR #77 (closed unmerged) run `36283399166` installed production requirements, syntax-checked `streamlit_app.py` and the complete Risk/E*TRADE production path, reran the simulated helper test, rendered every top-level tab plus repeated GEX/Risk routes and a second session through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** production implementation `04caa81866c911c7f46db007d652a6979e339274`; this changelog append is a documentation-only follow-up commit.
- **Lesson:** Keep live trading behind broker preview plus explicit confirmation, treat ambiguous placement responses as potentially successful until the broker Orders screen is checked, and never unlock a separately submitted protective stop from a partial fill.

## 2026-09-27 — Split Risk live orders into Parts 3/4 and eliminate overlap/duplicate rendering

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing live-order layout and account-selection flow only.
- **Exact production files changed in PR #78:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, and `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`; regressions updated in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` and added in `scripts/test_risk_order_layout_browser.py`.
- **What was broken:** The live-order section could render twice, including a stale disconnected copy above the active copy, and the Tactical Capacity warning could visually collide with the next orange section header. The live-order flow also did not present account choice as its own numbered step.
- **Root cause:** The first live-order implementation temporarily replaced `_v2._render_next_trade` during the v10 render. That indirect injection made the order block vulnerable to duplicate/stale fragment output. The compact Tactical Capacity warning used an inline markdown box without an explicit content-height/wrap contract.
- **What changed:** Removed the temporary `_render_next_trade` injection entirely. v2 now exposes one explicit optional post-Part-2 callback, v9 forwards it, and v10 renders Parts 3/4 exactly once through that callback outside the Part 2 panel. Part 3 is `3. PICK E*TRADE ACCOUNT` with a real E*TRADE account selector. Account suffix 5474 is the default when present, while the user can choose another available account before submission. Once an entry/uncertain submission exists, the order account is locked so the protective stop cannot accidentally move to another account. Part 4 is `4. REVIEW + SEND ORDER` and retains the preview/final-confirmation/live-entry/full-fill/separate-protective-stop workflow.
- **Overlap rule:** The live-order panel uses normal document flow only: no negative margins, no absolute positioning, and no fixed content heights. The Tactical Capacity warning now uses a dedicated content-height, wrap-safe HTML class with visible overflow and bottom spacing. Parts 3/4 have a compact positive gap and render outside the Part 2 container.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Raj account ending 5474 remains the default order account when available; manual account selection is allowed before submission. Live BUY LIMIT entry still requires E*TRADE preview plus explicit confirmation. The protective SELL STOP remains locked until a full fill and stays in the same account as the entry. Partial fills remain blocked. Ambiguous broker responses continue to block automatic retries. Risk sizing formulas, Entry/Stop/MAX SHARES logic, Capital Source, quote behavior, and Risk Book behavior are unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/terminal_core.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/login UI, E*TRADE order transport, Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` / `src/trade_math.py`, ticker autocomplete, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, shared theme, Bull Debit, Muni, and other top-level features.

Temporary focused validation run `36294275682` passed syntax checks, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`, `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py`, the real Selenium geometry regression `scripts/test_risk_order_layout_browser.py`, all top-level tabs via `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. The browser regression verified exactly one Part 3 and one Part 4, Raj/5474 as the default order account, Tactical Capacity warning bottom <= Part 3 top, and account-picker bottom <= Part 4 top. The temporary validation workflow was removed; final PR-head architecture guard run `36294346406` passed.

- **PR / implementation head:** PR #78, branch `fix/risk-order-parts-3-4-overlap-20260927`; final pre-changelog implementation head `ffc557e071f1ef5995997bf53025b5060e9dcfb8`.
- **Deployment status:** validated and ready to merge; production merge was not completed from this chat.
- **Lesson:** Do not inject Risk order UI by temporarily replacing the Part 2 renderer. Use an explicit one-way render callback and geometry-test every dense warning/header/control boundary that can wrap.

## 2026-09-27 — Link Risk E*TRADE account picker and Order Account picker bidirectionally

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing account-selection synchronization only.

When the user changes Part 3 `Order Account`, its callback maps that account key back to the matching index in `etrade_accounts` and updates `risk_sizing_account` before the fragment reruns. Thus either selector immediately drives the other. The existing submitted-order safety lock remains authoritative: once a live/uncertain entry or stop owns an account, both selectors are forced back to that locked account until the order workflow is cleared.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Raj account ending 5474 remains the default when available. The two selectors must never disagree. Changing either selector before submission must update the other. A submitted live-order workflow must stay on its original account. Changing account clears stale preview/review state but does not alter Risk formulas or live-order transport.

## 2026-09-27 — Integrate protective-stop send handoff into Risk Part 4

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Part 4 live-order protection handoff only.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`. Focused regression coverage changed in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`; the architecture guard label was updated in `scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **What was missing:** Part 4 submitted the reviewed BUY LIMIT, then required a separate later stop-review/confirmation sequence. Raj wanted Review + Send Order to carry the planned stop loss through the same protected workflow.
- **Root cause / broker constraint:** E*TRADE's public order API does not provide a broker-linked contingent/bracket submission for this path, so the protective SELL STOP cannot safely be placed before the shares exist. A separate stop order must follow a confirmed fill.
- **What changed:** The entry final review now shows both the DAY BUY LIMIT and the exact planned GTC stop. After the entry is submitted, one explicit `CHECK FULL FILL + SEND PROTECTIVE STOP` action rechecks E*TRADE Orders. If and only if E*TRADE reports the complete entry fill, the terminal previews the same-account, same-quantity GTC SELL STOP and submits it during that user-authorized action when the broker preview is clean. If E*TRADE returns preview messages, placement pauses and requires explicit message review before send. The stop order ID is shown immediately without a forced fragment rerun.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Raj account ending 5474 remains the default when available; the main Risk account and Part 3 Order Account remain synchronized; a submitted workflow stays locked to its original account; BUY entry remains LIMIT/DAY; protective stop remains SELL STOP/GTC at the reviewed Stop Loss; open/partial fills never submit the full planned stop; ambiguous entry/stop placement responses continue to block automatic retries; no background or unattended stop submission is introduced.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_client.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` / `src/trade_math.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/login UI, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, Option Book, navigation, theme, Bull Debit, Muni, and unrelated top-level features.

Architecture Guard run `36296114457` also passed on the validated implementation head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **PR / validated implementation head:** PR #80, branch `fix/risk-protective-stop-send-20260927`; validated production-code head `1ae50c1df2f4cbc632ed228858cf5a9459334606`.
- **Lesson:** Keep the protective stop tied to the exact reviewed entry context, require a broker-confirmed full fill before submission, and use one explicit user action for the post-fill preview/place handoff instead of pretending the public API supports a linked bracket order.

## 2026-09-27 — Use #444444 editable fields and add Risk pending-order cancellation

- **Features changed:** terminal-wide editable-field appearance; E*TRADE Risk live-order status/cancellation controls.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/theme.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, and `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`. Production ownership documentation was updated in `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`; focused regressions were updated in `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py`, `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py`, and `scripts/validate_architecture.py`.
- **What was requested:** Replace the just-added `#555555` editable-box fill with `#444444` everywhere in the terminal, and add a compact bottom section where pending E*TRADE orders can be reviewed and cancelled.
- **Broker/API constraint:** E*TRADE's public API exposes live order statuses and a Cancel Order endpoint, but does not expose Power E*TRADE broker-saved draft orders. The new bottom panel therefore shows live `OPEN`, `INDIVIDUAL_FILLS`, and `CANCEL_REQUESTED` orders for the selected/synchronized Risk account.
- **What changed:** Shared enabled editable fields now use `#444444` through `src/theme.py`. Risk Sizing now renders `5. PENDING / OPEN E*TRADE ORDERS` after Parts 3/4. It loads the selected account's open/partial/cancel-requested live orders, displays order ID, status, action, quantity, symbol, price type/price, and term, and includes a refresh control. `OPEN` and `INDIVIDUAL_FILLS` orders have a two-step cancel flow; `CANCEL_REQUESTED` is display-only to prevent duplicate cancellation requests. Cancelling a live SELL STOP shows an explicit protective-stop warning before confirmation.
- **Cancellation transport:** `ETradeClient.cancel_order()` sends the documented `PUT /v1/accounts/{accountIdKey}/orders/cancel` request with `{"CancelOrderRequest":{"orderId":...}}`. A successful HTTP response is reported as **cancel request submitted**, never as final cancellation; the user is told to refresh and verify broker status. Order-list HTTP 204 responses are treated as an empty result only for List Orders.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Main Risk account and Part 3 Order Account stay synchronized; Raj ending 5474 remains the default when available; BUY entry remains LIMIT/DAY; protective stop remains SELL STOP/GTC and full-fill gated; partial fills never send the full planned stop; uncertain broker submissions still block blind retries; Option Book remains preview-only and cannot place or cancel live orders.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` / `src/trade_math.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/login UI, Holdings, GEX, Schwab Risk, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, `src/terminal_core.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, and `.streamlit/config.toml`.
- **Tests performed:** PR #82 deep validation run `36297548925` passed production requirements install, syntax checks for all changed Python paths, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including exact CancelOrderRequest transport and two-click UI cancellation, `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py` including pending-order normalization, all top-level tabs through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. Architecture Guard run `36297548869` passed architecture validation, Risk live-order helper safety checks, and workflow validation.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Validated implementation head:** `89208a8328d7ef486022031760a639ee12eb068c` before removal of the temporary validation workflow.
- **Lesson:** Keep terminal-wide input color in `src/theme.py`; keep Risk cancellation explicit and account-scoped; treat E*TRADE cancellation as asynchronous until the broker order status confirms it.

## 2026-09-27 — Automatic Risk fill watcher + persistent protection log

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing post-entry protection monitoring and audit trail.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py` and the proven cross-tab background-hook dependency `streamlit_app.py`. Production ownership/guard documentation changed in `src/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `scripts/validate_architecture.py`; focused regressions changed in `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` and `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py`.
- **What Raj requested:** Make the post-entry protection workflow automatic and keep a durable log that remembers which filled entries still need protective stop-loss orders.
- **Broker/API constraint discovered before coding:** E*TRADE's current Developer Terms define Algorithmic / Automated Order Generation to include logic that generates, initiates, routes, submits, modifies, cancels, or manages an order without a discrete, contemporaneous affirmative instruction for that specific order, and Section 4.9 prohibits that behavior. Therefore the terminal may automatically monitor order status, but it may not automatically preview/place the protective stop after a later fill.
- **What changed:** Once the reviewed BUY LIMIT is successfully placed, Terminal 8 immediately arms a browser-persistent protection-watch record containing the exact account, entry order ID, symbol, quantity, entry, planned stop, timestamps, status, and audit events. A hidden Streamlit fragment polls E*TRADE List Orders every 5 seconds while Terminal 8 is open and a live session is available, even if another top-level tab is active. The watcher never extends user inactivity/session life. It tracks `ARMED`, `WAITING_FILL`, `PARTIAL_FILL`, `READY_TO_SEND`, entry terminal states, `STOP_SENT`, and `STOP_UNCERTAIN`.
- **Ready-to-send behavior:** A broker-confirmed complete fill changes the persistent record to `READY_TO_SEND`. Risk Part 4 then shows the exact planned SELL STOP / GTC and a `REVIEW + SEND READY PROTECTIVE STOP` control. That user click rechecks the exact entry again, performs the E*TRADE stop preview, and—if clean—places the stop during the same contemporaneous user action. Preview messages still require explicit review/confirmation before placement.
- **Persistent recovery:** The protection watch ledger is stored in browser local storage using the existing zero-height Risk browser-state mechanism. Armed/ready entries can be restored after refresh/redeploy, and Part 6 `PROTECTION WATCH LOG` displays the recent audit history. Monitoring pauses when the terminal/browser or live E*TRADE session is unavailable and resumes when the state is restored.
- **Critical safety boundary:** `maybe_auto_watch_risk_entries()` is read-only. It may call `list_orders()` only; architecture CI now fails if that unattended watcher contains preview, place, cancel, or protective-stop placement calls. Automatic polling is not allowed to submit a trade.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** BUY entry remains explicit Preview + confirmation + Place. Main Risk account and Part 3 Order Account remain synchronized; Raj ending 5474 remains the default when available. Full-quantity stop remains blocked on partial fills. Protective stop remains SELL STOP/GTC at the reviewed Stop Loss. Uncertain broker responses remain blocked from blind retry. Part 5 pending/open order cancellation remains two-step. Option Book remains preview-only.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/etrade_client.py`, Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` / `src/trade_math.py`, E*TRADE OAuth UI, Holdings, GEX logic, Schwab Risk, navigation component, Bull Debit, Muni, `src/terminal_core.py`, and terminal-wide theme.
- **Tests performed:** PR #83 deep validation run `36298476898` passed production requirements install, syntax checks, `scripts/test_risk_production_ui.py` including watch arming / ready-stop send / stop-log completion, `scripts/test_risk_live_order_helpers.py` including READY/PARTIAL watch transitions, Selenium `scripts/test_risk_order_layout_browser.py`, all top-level tabs through `scripts/test_tab_layout.py`, and `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`. Architecture Guard run `36298476913` also passed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Validated implementation head:** `c5c3cfd610493b7ab6ee53b30fad02452f2d5463` before removal of the temporary validation workflow.
- **Lesson:** Automate read-only fill detection and durable protection tracking, but keep every later E*TRADE order submission behind a contemporaneous, specific user instruction as required by the broker's current developer terms.

## 2026-09-27 — Add live E*TRADE Performance / ITD-coverage workspace

- **Feature changed:** New top-level E*TRADE Performance workspace with a live scorecard and cumulative P&L chart.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** new \`src/performance_ui.py\`; read-only transaction transport in \`src/etrade_client.py\`; required top-level routing in \`streamlit_app.py\` and \`src/tab_bar_v4.py\`; ownership/source-of-truth updates in \`src/ARCHITECTURE.md\` and \`src/production_manifest.py\`. Regression coverage added in \`scripts/test_performance_ui.py\` and the permanent architecture workflow/validator.
- **What was missing:** Raj's Terminal had current holdings/position gain data but no dedicated account-performance workspace for TODAY, MTD, YTD, and ALL-TIME/ITD coverage, no win-rate/profit-factor/drawdown scorecard, and no live cumulative performance chart.
- **Root cause:** E*TRADE's portfolio response is current-position performance, not a complete account-level inception return. E*TRADE's transaction endpoint provides a rolling history window, so a trustworthy terminal cannot infer pre-window closed trades or silently label current-position total gain as full ITD.
- **What was changed:** Added a compact Bloomberg-orange/black Performance tab with an E*TRADE account selector, account-value/TODAY/current-unrealized/live-update KPI strip, the requested PERFORMANCE / TODAY / MTD / YTD / ALL-TIME table, and a Plotly cumulative net-P&L line. The active tab force-refreshes current portfolio/balance every 30 seconds and lightweight-syncs the most recent seven days of transactions so newly closed trades enter the chart without a page reload. Two years of available transaction history are initially paged and session-cached. Realized trade statistics use FIFO lot matching with brokerage fees and option multipliers; dividend/interest cash is included. ALL-TIME adds current unrealized P&L to available-history realized P&L. A permanent coverage strip shows the earliest loaded E*TRADE transaction and states that ALL-TIME is partial if the account predates the API window. Return is explicitly cost-basis return rather than TWR/XIRR, and Max Drawdown is explicitly realized-P&L drawdown versus current account value.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Performance is strictly read-only and must never call Preview/Place/Cancel order methods. Keep the 30-second live market/transaction refresh while the tab is active, manual REFRESH PERFORMANCE, exact scorecard columns/rows, visible history-coverage warning, and feature-local compact orange/black styling. Never invent missing pre-window transactions or relabel E*TRADE current-position total gain as complete ITD.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk Sizing UI/formulas/watch logic, GEX, Holdings, E*TRADE OAuth/session UI, Option Book/Orders behavior, Schwab Risk Sizing, Bull Debit, Muni, shared theme/config, \`src/terminal_core.py\`, and E*TRADE live-order behavior.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions Performance Validation run 36299995645 on implementation head 271fa1ac964ca54c1b1e2e47730d142fe5454f25 installed production requirements on Python 3.14, syntax-checked the affected production/test files, passed \`scripts/test_performance_ui.py\` (FIFO P&L, fees, dividends, win rate, profit factor, return, drawdown, live-refresh/read-only source contracts), rendered every production top-level tab through \`scripts/test_tab_layout.py\`, and passed \`python scripts/validate_architecture.py\`. Terminal Architecture Guard run 36299995785 also passed on the same implementation head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS on the validated implementation head. The final clean PR head is revalidated after temporary-workflow removal, permanent regression-hook addition, and this changelog append.
- **Commit SHA:** validated Performance implementation head 271fa1ac964ca54c1b1e2e47730d142fe5454f25; permanent Performance regression hook 1810580033424e8e205328e6eba105bb9c3f3083.
- **Lesson:** Account performance must distinguish broker current-position gain from historical account performance. Use live broker data where it is authoritative, reconstruct only what transaction history actually supports, and make history coverage/formula limitations visible instead of filling gaps with assumptions.

## 2026-09-27 — Smart E*TRADE Rebalance Portfolio v1

- **Feature changed:** Rebalance Portfolio moved from an empty top-level shell to a read-only smart E*TRADE portfolio rebalancer.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** new `src/rebalance_portfolio_ui.py`; required import/header/dispatch wiring in `streamlit_app.py`. Ownership/source-of-truth records updated in `src/production_manifest.py` and `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`; permanent safety contracts added in `scripts/validate_architecture.py`; focused regression coverage added in `scripts/test_rebalance_portfolio.py`.
- **What was missing / root cause:** The Rebalance tab existed only as `elif active_tab == "REBALANCE PORTFOLIO": pass`, so it had no portfolio data, targets, bands, drift math, loss handling, cash-aware funding, persistence, or action plan.
- **What changed:** Added a compact Bloomberg-orange/black Rebalance workspace that intentionally reuses the production E*TRADE Risk Sizing account selector state, holdings normalization, account balance/cash source, classification engine, and persisted LONG-TERM intent memory without modifying Risk Sizing. Each holding has an editable target weight and drift band persisted per hashed account key in browser local storage. New holdings seed their target to their current account weight so no trade is invented until Raj defines a target. Default mode is `TO BAND`, which only acts after a band breach and moves the holding just back inside the band; `TO TARGET` is optional. The engine supports Tactical/Long-Term default bands, a minimum-dollar trade threshold, loss-review threshold, hard maximum position percentage, target cash, cash-first buy funding, required-trim proceeds, funding-aware prorated buys, CSV export, and HOLD / ADD / TRIM / REVIEW LOSS / LONG-TERM TRIM REVIEW / HARD LIMIT REVIEW / REVIEW SHORT states.
- **Loss / long-term behavior:** An underweight holding at or below the configured loss threshold is `REVIEW LOSS` and receives no automatic ADD proposal. A LONG-TERM overweight is surfaced as a review item rather than silently treated as a routine tactical trim. V1 is long-only; negative-market-value/short positions are review-only.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Rebalance and E*TRADE Risk Sizing share the same `risk_sizing_account` selection and LONG-TERM intent source. Targets/bands must remain persistent per account. `TO BAND` remains the low-turnover default. Excess cash above target cash is used before selling solely to fund underweights; required trim proceeds may then fund eligible adds. Trades below the configured minimum are suppressed. Target totals above 100% are blocked in V1.
- **Safety boundary:** Rebalance v1 is analysis-only. It may read E*TRADE accounts, portfolio and balances, but it must never preview, place, change, or cancel an order. Architecture CI now rejects `preview_order(`, `place_order(`, or `cancel_order(` inside the Rebalance owner.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** all Risk Sizing production owner/support files, Risk formulas in `src/risk_sizing.py` / `src/trade_math.py`, `src/etrade_client.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/session UI, Holdings, Performance, GEX, Schwab Risk, Bull Debit, navigation implementation, shared theme, Muni dormant code, Option Book, Orders, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary Rebalance Validation run `36344662032` passed production dependency install, Python syntax checks, focused deterministic Rebalance regression cases, `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, and `python scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across every enabled top-level tab. Terminal Architecture Guard run `36344666596` passed on the validated implementation head. The focused test verifies TO-BAND arithmetic, cash-first funding from required trim proceeds, loss-review blocking, LONG-TERM review, and minimum-trade suppression. An earlier validation failure was isolated to the standalone test's Python import path and corrected without changing production math.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Validated implementation head:** `e38f545b3c3e4cb385b63c21277fae5cf47718cb`; temporary validation workflow removed in cleanup commit `4c9a2bc93ac2daeaf4eef1a1a836029a10bfac9a`. Final production merge/deployment SHA is recorded after post-push verification.
- **Lesson:** A smart rebalancer should separate deterministic allocation math from order execution, minimize turnover with drift bands, treat losses and LONG-TERM intent as explicit review constraints, and reuse Risk Sizing's read-only broker/book context without editing or coupling to its live-order workflow.

## 2026-09-27 — Rebalance theme alignment, saved per-ticker bands, tolerance colors

- **Feature changed:** Rebalance Portfolio presentation + per-ticker tolerance configuration only.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/rebalance_portfolio_ui.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_rebalance_portfolio.py` and `scripts/test_rebalance_connected_ui.py`.
- **What was broken / hard to read:** Rebalance used a weaker one-off visual layer than E*TRADE Risk Sizing, some text could appear too dark against the terminal background, tolerance state was not visually obvious, and each ticker only had one symmetric `Band +/- %` instead of independently editable lower/upper tolerance limits.
- **Root cause:** Rebalance v1 had its own minimal CSS and a symmetric-band state schema. It did not reuse the clearer Risk-terminal visual language locally, and its editor exposed only one deviation value per symbol.
- **What changed:** Rebalance now uses feature-scoped Risk-style orange borders, black terminal surfaces, white/orange editable text, compact aligned inputs/buttons, and no black feature text. Every ticker now has independently editable and auto-saved `LOWER %`, `TARGET %`, and `UPPER %` values. Existing saved legacy `Band +/- %` settings automatically migrate to equivalent lower/upper values without losing the prior target. The plan adds an explicit `TOLERANCE` state; IN TOLERANCE is highlighted Bloomberg green (`#4af6c3`) and OUT OF TOLERANCE is highlighted red (`#ff433d`). P&L and proposed trade direction also receive matching green/red emphasis for scanability.
- **Memory behavior that must remain:** Targets, lower bands, upper bands, rebalance mode, default Tactical/Long-Term bands, minimum trade, loss-review trigger, and hard maximum are browser-persisted per hashed E*TRADE account key. ## 2026-09-27 — Permanent CUSIP / fixed-income LONG-TERM protection

- **Feature changed:** Shared Risk classification + Risk Book presentation + Rebalance sell protection for CUSIP/fixed-income holdings.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, and `src/rebalance_portfolio_ui.py`. Focused tests updated in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py` and `scripts/test_rebalance_portfolio.py`.
- **What was broken / incomplete:** The shared classifier already treated an explicit populated `CUSIP` field or known fixed-income security type as LONG-TERM, but some E*TRADE positions can arrive with the CUSIP in the `Symbol` field instead. A holding such as `337158EJ4` could therefore miss the structural LONG-TERM rule if E*TRADE did not populate the separate CUSIP field. Rebalance also previously allowed a LONG-TERM overweight review row to carry a negative raw trade/proposed sell amount.
- **Root cause:** Identifier recognition depended on the separate `CUSIP` field/security type instead of also recognizing a CUSIP-shaped Symbol. Rebalance distinguished ordinary LONG-TERM review from TACTICAL trimming, but it did not have a stronger never-trim class for structural CUSIP/fixed-income holdings.
- **What changed:** Added the shared `is_protected_long_term_holding()` rule in `src/risk_sizing.py`. It protects known fixed-income security types, any nonempty explicit CUSIP field, and 9-character CUSIP-shaped identifiers delivered in `Symbol` (including `337158EJ4`). Protected positions classify as `LONG-TERM` with reason `CUSIP / FIXED INCOME // PROTECTED`. In the Risk Book, their LONG-TERM checkbox is automatically checked and disabled, and protected identifiers are treated as LONG-TERM in Risk Book sorting/export even without a manual override. In Rebalance, an overweight/hard-max protected position becomes `PROTECTED LONG-TERM // HOLD` with reason `CUSIP / FIXED INCOME // NEVER TRIM`; its raw trade and proposed sell remain exactly $0, so it never supplies trim proceeds to fund other buys.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** This protection is code-level and does not depend on browser memory or a manual checkbox. Normal ticker LONG-TERM overrides remain user-controlled and browser-persisted exactly as before. Ordinary manually/threshold-classified LONG-TERM equities can still use the existing review behavior; the absolute never-trim rule applies to protected CUSIP/fixed-income identifiers. Rebalance remains analysis-only and may not preview/place/cancel orders.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/theme.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, Holdings presentation, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client transport, GEX, Performance, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Option Book, Orders, and live Risk order submission/watcher logic.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary CUSIP Long-Term Validation final run `36371988319` passed syntax checks; exact `337158EJ4` classification with blank CUSIP field and misleading `EQ` type; explicit-CUSIP protection; normal ticker non-protection; Risk export/sort behavior; protected Rebalance no-trim behavior including a hard-max breach; production Risk route; connected Rebalance route; `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`; and `python scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across every enabled top-level tab.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Validated implementation head before workflow cleanup:** `6894b6a209ff6b8c5f68e86001ef38804789917f`; temporary validation workflow removed in cleanup commit `2d2d2ed9b42166ea3273d44a279916406035287d`. Final production merge/deployment SHA is recorded after post-push verification.
- **Lesson:** Structural identifiers must be classified from the actual holding identifier, not only from optional broker metadata. Protected fixed-income/CUSIP positions are permanent LONG-TERM and must never be used as Rebalance trim/sell funding.
