## 2026-09-27 — Reboot-safe Risk + Rebalance browser memory

- **Feature changed:** Persistence reliability for E*TRADE Risk Sizing edits / LONG-TERM checkmarks and Rebalance targets, bands, and settings across Streamlit app restarts/redeploys.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`, `src/rebalance_portfolio_ui.py`, `src/components/risk_book_state_v1/index.html`, and `src/components/risk_intent_state_v1/index.html`. Regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py` and `scripts/test_rebalance_portfolio.py`.
- **What was broken:** Browser-backed state existed, but a fresh Streamlit process could build fresh/default server state before the asynchronous browser component had finished returning localStorage. Under equal/near revisions, that fresh state could replace saved Risk/Rebalance values after a reboot, making edits or LONG-TERM checks appear forgotten.
- **Root cause:** The browser components mixed read/reconcile/write behavior in one pass and Python did not require a completed browser hydration handshake before allowing a fresh process to write. Risk settings also restored only when widget keys were absent, so first-pass widget defaults could block later browser restoration.
- **What changed:** Added explicit `mode="read"` / `mode="write"` persistence handshakes. Fresh processes cannot write browser state until the browser has explicitly answered with either saved state or an empty-state response. Risk Book uses a provisional-session marker so current-session behavior still works while browser hydration is pending, but the next pass still gives persisted browser state priority. Risk editable settings are restored once per saved revision/fingerprint, including the capital-source toggle. Risk LONG-TERM intent has its own per-account hydration gate. Rebalance has the same per-account hydration-before-write rule and uses revision + saved timestamp when hydrating its setting widgets.
- **Multiple saved instances:** Both browser persistence components now keep the current copy plus **four rolling backup generations** in localStorage. On load, the newest valid revision among the primary/backup copies is selected. A malformed/missing primary can therefore recover from a prior generation instead of silently starting over.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Edits save automatically on normal Streamlit reruns; there is no separate Save button. Risk LONG-TERM intent remains account-scoped and still removes a normal ticker's manual override after that ticker disappears from holdings, per the existing rule. Protected CUSIP/fixed-income LONG-TERM behavior remains code-level and independent of browser memory. Rebalance settings remain account-scoped. This persistence stores terminal UI/state only in the same browser profile; E*TRADE OAuth tokens remain server-memory only and are intentionally not written to browser storage.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** E*TRADE OAuth/session semantics, `src/session_persistence.py`, Risk formulas/math, live Risk order preview/place/cancel/watch workflow, `src/theme.py`, `streamlit_app.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, Holdings, GEX, Performance, navigation, Bull Debit, Muni, Option Book, and Orders.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary Reboot Persistence Validation final run `36373645573` passed Python syntax, Risk memory/LONG-TERM regression checks, Rebalance persistence/math checks, production Risk UI, connected Rebalance UI, reboot-guard regression, `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, and `python scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across every enabled top-level tab. Pull-request Terminal Architecture Guard run `36373702618` also passed.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Validated implementation head before cleanup:** `df8ad461c9f53238cfe2030c4177a302435a2b54`; temporary workflow removed in cleanup commit `777ad11201dfcdd41dc3310806762f9c94d83598`. Final production merge/deployment SHA is recorded after post-push verification.
- **Lesson:** Never let a newly restarted Streamlit session write defaults into browser persistence until the browser has first completed an explicit restore handshake. Keep rolling browser backups for user-edited portfolio policy state.

A real reboot creates a new session and restores saved values again, but subsequent autosave revisions in that live session no longer overwrite active widget edits. No quote-loading, autocomplete/company display, sizing math, or broker-order logic was changed.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Reboot persistence still restores saved Risk settings on a new app/session; the ticker remains the only editable ticker control; changing the ticker must normalize to uppercase, update the company display/quote symbol, preserve the E*TRADE-first/Yahoo-fallback quote path, and allow Entry/Stop reseeding through the existing production flow. - **Lesson:** Persisted defaults require a versioned one-time migration; do not make the migration unconditional or it will overwrite a later intentional user override.

## 2026-09-28 — Republish GEX raw TXT on every refresh and bypass stale raw-link cache

- **Feature changed:** GEX raw TXT bridge refresh behavior for E*TRADE and CBOE.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py` and `scripts/test_gex_dte.py`.
- **What was broken:** A refreshed GEX view could show the newly selected DTE while the raw TXT button still appeared to show an older bridge snapshot. CBOE refresh already republished its source-isolated bridge, but the button reused the identical raw GitHub URL and could therefore reopen a cached copy. E*TRADE foreground/per-ticker refreshes and DTE-save refreshes also did not explicitly republish `bridge/latest_gex.txt`; E*TRADE bridge publication was mainly tied to completed background Refresh All.
- **Root cause:** The raw TXT controls had stable URLs with no cache-busting query value, and the E*TRADE single-ticker refresh path updated the in-memory result map without invoking the existing GitHub bridge publisher.
- **What changed:** Both `E*TRADE TXT` and `CBOE TXT` controls now append a fresh cache-busting query value each render so opening the raw file requests the newest GitHub branch content. E*TRADE foreground ticker refreshes now republish the current full E*TRADE result map to `bridge/latest_gex.txt` immediately after a successful refresh. Saving a per-ticker DTE with `SAVE & REFRESH TICKER` does the same. Existing E*TRADE background Refresh All publication remains unchanged. Existing CBOE all-ticker and single-ticker refreshes continue to republish `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt` after their result map is saved.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep E*TRADE and CBOE bridge files strictly source-isolated. E*TRADE refreshes may update only `bridge/latest_gex.txt`; CBOE refreshes may update only `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`. Do not change GEX formulas, DTE calculation semantics, OAuth/session behavior, refresh concurrency/rate limiting, or source-selection behavior while maintaining TXT publication.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py` calculations, `src/gex_github_bridge.py` transport semantics, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, E*TRADE OAuth/client/session code, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, Option Book, navigation, shared theme, authentication, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Temporary GitHub Actions run `36428812643` installed production requirements and passed `python scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`, `python scripts/test_gex_dte.py`, `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, and `python scripts/test_tab_layout.py`. Standard Terminal Architecture Guard run `36428549700` also passed the production architecture checks, deployed Streamlit health check, and rendered frontend check.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Implementation commits:** CBOE stale-link cache bypass `90b9fdbb28abacca027acd7630ab9c99599ef311`; all-refresh E*TRADE TXT publication `4a9dbb070f91c69b10fbd035bd4393e4b5412252`; regression coverage `2416128815d0615e748ee57fba8cca53de37c439` and `9f0a23cc419cf2f7342c55376b9e721e2247e8ef`.
- **Lesson:** A successful market-data refresh and a fresh raw-link view are separate concerns. Republish the appropriate source bridge whenever its result map changes, and use a cache-busted raw link so the browser/CDN cannot make a newly published file look stale.



## 2026-09-28 — Vertical Options live multi-account order ticket

- **Feature changed:** New Vertical Options top-level workspace for fast E*TRADE call/put debit-vertical order entry.

Ticket widgets run inside an `st.fragment` so value changes rerun only the ticket instead of the whole terminal.

## 2026-09-28 — Vertical Options account-chip text contrast

- **What changed:** Added a Vertical Options-only BaseWeb tag override that forces selected-account chip text and the remove icon to black while preserving orange text on dark unselected/input surfaces.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Selected account chips must remain readable as black text/icons on orange; do not change the global theme or Risk Sizing styling to solve this local Vertical Options issue.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk Sizing, Option Book, OAuth/session, GEX, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, navigation, `src/theme.py`, `src/terminal_core.py`, order logic, and option calculations.
- **Tests performed:** Production route re-traced; exact committed CSS re-fetched; Vertical Options syntax/payload regression tests passed in CI; architecture validator passed; production Streamlit health check passed; deployed frontend Selenium check confirmed no Streamlit “Oh no” page.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS — GitHub Actions `Terminal Architecture Guard` run `36483524405`.
- **Commit SHA:** `ff68ddd`, `12677a7` plus this changelog commit.
- **Lesson:** When a feature uses orange filled BaseWeb multiselect tags, explicitly set the tag label and remove icon to black inside the feature scope so the global/input orange text rule cannot erase the selection text.

## 2026-10-02 — GEX ticker add actually persists beyond 50 + Enter submit

Existing yfinance stock/ETF lookup remains the validation/resolution source.

## 2026-10-03 — Risk LONG-TERM checkbox/classification state reconciliation

- **Feature changed:** E*TRADE Risk Sizing Risk Book LONG-TERM manual intent overrides and regression coverage.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`; `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml` now executes that focused Risk test plus the existing top-level tab smoke check on production pushes.
- **What was broken:** A native LONG-TERM checkbox could visibly remain checked while the same row still rendered as `TACTICAL` and continued contributing to CURRENT TACTICAL / TACTICAL ROOM math. This created the exact contradictory state visible in the Risk Book: checked manual intent in the first column, but tactical sleeve text and percentages in the second column.
- **Root cause:** Risk maintained both native Streamlit widget state and account-scoped persisted ticker intent. The callback was the normal synchronization path, but the render path applied the persisted override dictionary before defensively reconciling an already-mounted checkbox value. A Streamlit/browser-state timing mismatch could therefore leave the visible widget and classification model temporarily or persistently out of sync. The focused Risk intent regression script also existed outside the permanent Architecture Guard, so this UI/model mismatch was not continuously exercised by CI.
- **What changed:** Added `_reconcile_visible_long_term_widget_state()` before classification/summary rendering. For ordinary ticker rows, a visible checked widget now repairs a stale account override to LONG-TERM before `_apply_intent_overrides()`; a visible unchecked widget removes a stale manual override. Protected CUSIP/fixed-income rows are excluded because their LONG-TERM status remains code-level, not manual intent. Duplicate lots keep ticker-level intent: if their widget states briefly disagree during a click rerun, the existing callback remains authoritative and the reconciliation layer does not reverse it. Any repaired intent is persisted through the existing account-scoped browser state path before the browser writer runs.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Checking LONG-TERM must immediately make that ticker classify and summarize as LONG-TERM. Unchecking returns it to the normal threshold classifier. Automatic threshold-qualified LONG-TERM equities may still have an unchecked manual-override box because that checkbox means manual intent, not automatic classification. CUSIP/fixed-income identifiers remain permanently LONG-TERM with a checked/disabled control. Duplicate lots remain ticker-level intent. Browser persistence, account scoping, four backup generations, Risk formulas, and the live order workflow remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/risk_sizing.py`, `src/trade_math.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v9.py`, `src/risk_sizing_ui_v10.py`, ticker autocomplete, E*TRADE OAuth/client/session transport, live order preview/place/cancel/watch logic, Rebalance calculations/UI, Holdings, GEX, Performance, Vertical Options behavior, navigation, shared theme, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** GitHub Actions Terminal Architecture Guard run `37167764368` passed. It installed production requirements, syntax-checked `src/risk_sizing_ui_v2.py` and `scripts/test_risk_intent_override.py`, passed the focused LONG-TERM reconciliation regression (checked→LONG-TERM, unchecked→normal classifier, duplicate-lot conflict protection, protected-CUSIP exclusion), passed `python scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across enabled top-level tabs/second session, passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, Risk live-order safety helpers, Vertical Options payload/syntax tests, Performance read-only tests, workflow validation, deployed Streamlit health, and deployed-browser no-`Oh no` verification.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** production fix `7bf2e5a17b393395fa3d604d7b58c5c77824d767`; focused regression `9781bed021f03a01af78b5e23b9863473ae7bc6e`; permanent CI coverage `238ac70dbb2dafea13abccc647a5b1abca6c5d9a`.
- **Lesson:** For interactive policy controls, the visible native widget and the persisted model must never be allowed to disagree. Reconcile the mounted widget state before classification/math, but preserve callback authority for transient duplicate-lot conflicts and keep structural CUSIP protection separate from manual intent.

## 2026-10-03 — Multi-Mac Touch ID enrollment + biometric scan animation

- **Feature changed:** Terminal access / Touch ID behavior across multiple Macs/browsers and the lock-screen biometric visual.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/lock_screen_v2.py`, `src/components/lock_keypad_v2/index.html`; ownership rule updated in `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py` and permanently wired into `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`.
- **What was broken:** Opening Raj's Terminal on another Mac could immediately launch a WebAuthn sign-in chooser that offered an iPhone/iPad/Android device or external security key instead of that Mac's Touch ID. The new Mac also had no clean path to enroll its own platform credential because the server already believed Touch ID was registered.
- **Root cause:** The production lock treated the process-global runtime credential file as proof that the current browser owned the credential. It then sealed/saved that existing public record into any browser session, including a different Mac. That made a fresh Mac attempt authentication with another Mac/browser's passkey identity, which naturally pushed Chromium/macOS toward cross-device or security-key options. The server cache was also single-record, while the intended durable ownership boundary is the HMAC-sealed record stored in each browser profile.
- **What changed:** Touch ID ownership is now browser/Mac scoped. A lock session starts with no biometric credential until that browser restores its own HMAC-sealed localStorage record or successfully enrolls/authenticates a credential in that same session. The process-global runtime cache is no longer used to mark a fresh browser as enrolled and is never copied into an unproven browser. A new Mac now reports `NEW MAC / BROWSER`, asks for the access code once, then exposes `SET UP TOUCH ID ON THIS MAC`; existing enrolled browsers restore their own record and continue the normal automatic Touch ID challenge. Successful enrollment/authentication stores the verified record in session and only then refreshes that browser's sealed memory. Existing enrolled Macs therefore remain independent even if another Mac enrolls later.
- **Recovery behavior:** Added a compact `RE-ENROLL TOUCH ID ON THIS MAC` action for an enrolled browser whose local passkey was removed/replaced. It still requires the access code before creating a new platform credential.
- **Animation/UI:** Replaced the static lock icon with a compact Bloomberg-terminal-inspired `BIO UNIT // AUTH SCAN` module: orange grid/reticle, animated scan sweep, state readout (`CHECK MAC`, `NEW MAC`, `TOUCH READY`, `VERIFYING`, etc.), and green active/ready treatment. The animation is isolated to the lock component and honors `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **Security/privacy behavior that must remain:** Every new Mac/browser requires the terminal access code once before it can enroll a new Touch ID/passkey. Raw fingerprint/biometric data and passkey private keys never enter Raj's Terminal. Browser storage contains only the HMAC-sealed public WebAuthn verification record. WebAuthn still requires a platform/discoverable credential and user verification; external security keys are not accepted by the Touch ID shortcut.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/passkey_auth.py` cryptographic WebAuthn verification/sealing semantics, E*TRADE OAuth/session/client behavior, Risk Sizing, Rebalance, GEX, Holdings, Performance, Vertical Options, Option Book, navigation, shared theme, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Terminal Architecture Guard run `37169760563` passed. It syntax-checked the Touch ID production path, passed `scripts/test_touch_id_persistence.py` including multi-Mac/browser ownership assertions, validated the lock component JavaScript with `node --check`, passed `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, rendered all top-level tab smoke tests, passed Risk live-order, Vertical Options and Performance safety tests, validated workflow YAML, verified the deployed Streamlit health endpoint, and passed the real headless-Chrome no-`Oh no` frontend check. Native Touch ID hardware itself cannot be exercised by headless CI, so final physical-Mac biometric confirmation remains a real-device check.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** browser/Mac ownership fix `656e57e542d646f0e449b84ca082c9625c4fcf2d`; biometric animation + re-enrollment UI `021af7507ce608454bb39e0193e8850bd1fc766e`; focused regression `1ebc50af8e8a30cccb1aaf49e79019ffde127fc4`; architecture rule `1b18aa8c4bd0ed6b26db70b9cd6cb6a1f74bb15f`; permanent CI coverage `3580af5ad2b9fb772aa8cf093325a4282f9d2f99`.
- **Lesson:** A WebAuthn credential being known to the server does not mean the current browser/Mac owns its private key. Browser-scoped sealed credential memory must decide whether Touch ID is enrolled; otherwise a fresh Mac can be forced into a cross-device/security-key chooser instead of being allowed to enroll its own platform authenticator.



## 2026-10-04 — Repair CBOE TXT access and republish on ticker add

- **Feature changed:** GEX CBOE TXT access and watchlist-add refresh behavior.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`; focused regression coverage in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`.
- **What was broken:** The CBOE TXT button opened `raw.githubusercontent.com`, which could fail in the browser with an SSL record-length error. Separately, adding a valid ticker such as SUN persisted it to the GEX watchlist but immediately reran before any CBOE calculation/publish occurred, so `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt` could remain stale and omit the newly added ticker until Raj manually refreshed CBOE.
- **Root cause:** The UI link depended directly on the raw GitHub content host, while the watchlist-add path only saved state and reran. CBOE publication was correctly attached to CBOE refresh paths, but no post-add hook invoked that refresh for newly saved symbols.
- **What changed:** The CBOE TXT button now opens GitHub's normal file viewer (`github.com/.../blob/...?...plain=1`) with the existing cache-busting nonce, avoiding the failing raw-content browser endpoint. The GEX base renderer now exposes a source-specific post-add hook. Production GEX temporarily wires that hook inside `render_gex()`, refreshes only the newly added ticker(s) through the existing isolated CBOE batch path with `replace_all=False`, republishes the full current CBOE MASTER A6 TXT, reports the CBOE TXT update result in the add confirmation, and restores the hook in `finally`.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Adding a ticker must continue resolving through yfinance and persisting before any source refresh. A successful add must immediately attempt CBOE refresh/publication for only the newly added ticker(s), without replacing previously refreshed CBOE results. CBOE publication must continue writing only `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`. E*TRADE TXT behavior, DTE semantics, CBOE formulas, and manual CBOE refresh controls remain unchanged. Temporary production hooks must be restored in `finally`.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py` calculations, `src/gex_github_bridge.py` transport semantics, E*TRADE GEX math/publication logic, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, OAuth/session code, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, Vertical Options, navigation, shared theme, authentication, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Full PR diff audited; focused GEX/CBOE source-contract regression passed for post-save hook ordering, partial CBOE merge, TXT republish call, production hook install/restore, and non-raw CBOE browser URL. GitHub Actions Terminal Architecture Guard PR run `37236430579` passed, including `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, all top-level tab smoke coverage, Risk/Touch ID/Vertical Options/Performance regressions, and workflow validation.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS — PR run `37236430579`.
- **Commit SHAs:** base post-add hook `7c82c56e2b14c703d74e8d97c36bd329d8dcd543`; production CBOE refresh/link fix `2c421f8cc5c5fa2266cd62ebbcb66deb75a62f9a`; focused regression coverage `cbcd831a69fbb62bd7778bac33f3553ba10e246e`.
- **Lesson:** Persisting a GEX ticker and publishing its source bridge are separate operations. When a watchlist change should immediately affect a source TXT, route the new symbol through that source's existing refresh/publish path before rerun, and do not make the user-facing file control depend on a browser-host endpoint that is known to fail.


## 2026-10-04 — Preserve full CBOE TXT after fresh-process ticker add

- **Feature changed:** GEX CBOE post-add refresh safety across Streamlit process restarts.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`.
- **What was broken / risk found during post-merge audit:** CBOE results are held in a process-scoped cache. After a Streamlit reboot, that cache can be empty even though the persisted GitHub CBOE TXT still contains the prior universe. The first post-reboot ticker add from the immediately prior fix would refresh only the newly added ticker and then republish from the empty/new process snapshot, which could replace the full CBOE TXT with only the new ticker.
- **Root cause:** The post-add hook assumed the current process already contained all previously refreshed CBOE results. That assumption is false after a fresh process because `cboe_results()` is backed by `st.cache_resource`.
- **What changed:** Before deciding the post-add refresh scope, production GEX now compares the saved watchlist (excluding the newly added symbols) with the current process CBOE result map. If every pre-existing ticker is already present, only the newly added ticker(s) are refreshed and merged. If the process snapshot is incomplete or empty, the complete saved watchlist is refreshed and replaced before the TXT is republished, preventing the bridge from being truncated to the newly added symbol.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Normal in-session ticker adds should remain incremental when the current CBOE snapshot is complete. Fresh-process/incomplete snapshots must rebuild the saved watchlist before publication. CBOE TXT stays source-isolated, previously refreshed symbols must not disappear merely because the process restarted, and no E*TRADE/Risk/OAuth/theme behavior may be changed by this safeguard.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py` add-hook contract, `src/gex_cboe.py` calculations/cache design, `src/gex_github_bridge.py` transport, E*TRADE GEX, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, OAuth/session, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, Vertical Options, navigation, shared theme, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Focused fresh-process CBOE add regression passed for saved-watchlist inventory, current-snapshot coverage detection, incremental-vs-full refresh selection, and regression assertions. GitHub Actions Terminal Architecture Guard PR run `37236800456` passed, including `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, top-level tab smoke coverage, Touch ID/Risk/Vertical Options/Performance regressions, and workflow validation.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS — PR run `37236800456`.
- **Commit SHAs:** production safeguard `817d7ae186d251f9e7b301835ebbc31d0224fdc0`; focused regression coverage `74359d4dea4a27bf3647a1a7e666cbfb9197a16b`.
- **Lesson:** Any bridge publisher that serializes an in-memory result map must account for process restarts. Do not republish a persistent multi-ticker file from a partial fresh-process cache unless the missing prior universe has first been rebuilt.


## 2026-10-04 — Restore compact CBOE TradingView gamma text and use chart spot

- **Feature changed:** GEX CBOE TradingView / raw TXT serialization only.
- **Exact production file changed:** `src/gex_ui_v3.py`; focused regression coverage updated in `scripts/test_gex_cboe.py`.

Raj's TradingView workflow uses the chart's live market price, so that extra spot value and the surrounding metadata were unnecessary and made the gamma input harder to parse/use.

The complete multi-ticker payload still keeps exactly one outer opening and closing double quote.

The CBOE MASTER and single-ticker views use the same compact contract, and the old CBOE compact option that could reintroduce `SPOT` is no longer shown.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Keep `Ticker:` boundaries exact; keep `Source URL:`; keep the Pine-input label and gamma rows; do not reintroduce the CBOE `SPOT` row unless Raj explicitly asks. CBOE refreshes must continue publishing only `bridge/latest_gex_cboe.txt`. E*TRADE MASTER A6/raw TXT serialization must remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/gex_cboe.py` calculations, `src/gex_ui_v3_base.py`, `src/gex_github_bridge.py` transport, E*TRADE GEX serialization/calculations, `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`, OAuth/session code, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, Vertical Options, navigation, shared theme, `streamlit_app.py`, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

Standard Terminal Architecture Guard run `37259114758` also passed on the isolated implementation head.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Implementation head:** `5e2766994ed82f120b67aab02b643ba67ac730e1`.
- **Lesson:** The CBOE bridge is a TradingView transport, not an audit report. Keep only the fields Raj's workflow actually uses, and let TradingView's chart supply live spot instead of shipping a second spot value in the text payload.


## 2026-10-06 — Protective Puts tab from legacy Married Put engine

- **Feature changed:** Added a new top-level Protective Puts workspace using the legacy Married Put analytics from `G-enterpriseGroup/Praescientia-Auri`, adapted to Raj's Terminal.

- **What changed:** Added a compact Risk-Sizing-style Protective Puts tab. It uses live/current E*TRADE quote and option-chain transport, shows ticker/company/last price, editable purchase price, 100-share-lot protection sizing, one-expiration or all-expirations scanning, DTE, put bid/ask/last, volume, open interest, ASK/LAST put cost, ASK/LAST max loss, ASK breakeven, and CSV export. The legacy max-loss presentation was corrected to a positive loss amount: stock cost + put premium − strike floor value.

ASK is the conservative purchase-side reference; LAST is historical reference only. Styling remains local and follows the E*TRADE Risk Sizing compact orange/black visual contract without changing shared theme files.

## 2026-10-06 — Protective Puts market-date DTE accuracy follow-up

- **Feature changed:** Protective Puts DTE/default-expiration date handling.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/protective_puts_ui.py`.
- **What was broken:** Bare `date.today()` could use a UTC server date and move DTE/default-expiration selection one calendar day early during U.S. evening hours.
- **Root cause:** Streamlit Cloud server-local date is not guaranteed to match the U.S. options-market calendar date.
- **What changed:** Protective Puts now resolves the current date with `America/New_York` before calculating DTE, choosing the default expiration, and building scan rows.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** This is a Protective Puts-only accuracy rule; E*TRADE data sourcing, Risk Sizing, other tabs, and shared time/session behavior remain unchanged.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk Sizing, E*TRADE OAuth/client transport, Vertical Options, Option Book, GEX, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, navigation, shared theme, and `src/terminal_core.py`.

## 2026-10-07 — Protective Puts public-data fallback when E*TRADE is not live

- **Feature changed:** Protective Puts market-data sourcing.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** `src/protective_puts_ui.py`, new `src/protective_puts_sources.py`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `scripts/validate_architecture.py`, and `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`; focused regression coverage added in `scripts/test_protective_puts_sources.py`.
- **What was broken:** Protective Puts stopped at the connection boundary when E*TRADE was not live, even though the legacy married-put workflow could operate from public options data.
- **Root cause:** The production Protective Puts UI originally assumed a live E*TRADE client for quote, expiration, and put-chain retrieval.
- **What changed:** E*TRADE remains the primary source. When the E*TRADE client is absent or offline, Protective Puts now falls back to yfinance for quote/expirations/put chains. If yfinance fails, Protective Puts attempts direct Yahoo Finance HTML scraping for the same public data. Public rows are normalized into the same put-row shape used by the E*TRADE analytics, and the UI shows the quote/expiration/option-chain source so the active feed is visible.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** The source priority is E*TRADE -> yfinance -> Yahoo HTML. The fallback is Protective-Puts-only, read-only, and must never preview/place/change/cancel orders or alter E*TRADE OAuth/session behavior. E*TRADE data must always win when the live connector is available.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** Risk Sizing, E*TRADE OAuth UI/client transport, Vertical Options, Option Book, GEX, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, top navigation, shared theme, requirements, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Terminal Architecture Guard run `37632743968` passed architecture validation, every top-level tab smoke test, Risk/Vertical/Performance safety regressions, Protective Puts math tests, Protective Puts public-fallback tests including forced yfinance failure -> Yahoo HTML scraper success, workflow validation, production Streamlit health, and deployed-browser no-`Oh no` verification.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** fallback feature `e3013f4eeda1af710f6e232c3e7123af26a38c01`; CI coverage `24c925b8e5d641ec4632646e40956d262c320fad`; test-path correction `6a7abf88a6b23bf242b59c742fa23a316d1efed8`.
- **Lesson:** Protective Puts may degrade gracefully to public options data, but public-source parsing must remain isolated from broker plumbing and the UI must identify the active source.

## 2026-10-07 — Protective Puts control-row alignment cleanup

- **Feature changed:** Protective Puts compact control layout.

- **What was broken:** The primary input row and the expiration/DTE row did not read as one clean grid on wide screens. Quote-source text was left-floating under the entire row, and the expiration guidance sat too far away from the controls.
- **Root cause:** Protective Puts used mismatched column ratios between the first and second rows, plus unanchored captions outside the control grid.

- **Important behavior that must remain:** E*TRADE -> yfinance -> Yahoo HTML source priority, all Protective Puts calculations, 100-share coverage rule, read-only behavior, existing button behavior, and local Bloomberg-style theme remain unchanged.

## 2026-10-07 — Terminal-wide yfinance option-chain fallback

- **Feature changed:** Shared options-market fallback across the terminal.
- **Exact production file(s) changed:** new `src/yfinance_options.py`; `src/bull_debit_ui.py`; `src/vertical_options_ui.py`; `src/option_book_ui.py`; `src/gex_workspace_v2.py`; `src/gex_ui.py`; `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`; `scripts/validate_architecture.py`; and `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`. Focused regression coverage added in `scripts/test_yfinance_options.py`.
- **What was broken:** Outside Protective Puts, option-chain features depended on a live E*TRADE connector or prior cached brokerage chain data and could not continue from a public chain source when E*TRADE was disconnected.
- **Root cause:** Bull Debit Spread, Vertical Options, Option Book, and GEX each consumed the E*TRADE option-chain payload directly without a shared public compatibility layer.
- **What changed:** Added one read-only yfinance adapter that exposes E*TRADE-compatible quote, expiration, and option-chain payloads. E*TRADE remains primary whenever live. If the connector is not live, Bull Debit Spread, Vertical Options, Option Book, and GEX automatically use yfinance market data. The adapter preserves strike, bid, ask, last, volume, open interest, contract symbol, and implied volatility; it calculates Black-Scholes gamma and vega from yfinance IV for GEX compatibility. GEX source metadata now identifies yfinance when fallback data is active. Protective Puts keeps its existing yfinance-first public path plus its secondary Yahoo HTML fallback.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Source priority is live E*TRADE first, yfinance only when E*TRADE is not live. Public fallback is market-data-only and must never enable E*TRADE Preview/Place/Change/Cancel. Vertical Options and Option Book must keep broker actions disabled until the live connector returns. GEX must label yfinance-derived IV/Greeks accurately rather than claiming E*TRADE.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** E*TRADE OAuth/session implementation, `src/etrade_client.py`, Risk Sizing, Holdings, Performance, Rebalance, navigation, shared theme, requirements, and `src/terminal_core.py`.
- **Tests performed:** Terminal Architecture Guard run `37640930000` passed architecture validation, all top-level tab smoke tests, existing Risk/Vertical/Performance/Protective Puts regressions, new terminal-wide yfinance option fallback regression, workflow validation, production Streamlit health, and deployed-browser no-`Oh no` verification.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS.
- **Commit SHA:** `8da9d9b29cd950b39e2640dcfc0e24ecac841608`.
- **Lesson:** Keep public options fallback centralized in one read-only compatibility adapter. Do not duplicate per-feature yfinance parsing, and never let a public-data fallback cross the broker-action safety boundary.

## 2026-10-10 — New State Street historical Heatmaps tab

- **Feature changed:** New top-level HEATMAPS analytics workspace, sector performance and saved custom-ticker watchlist.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/heatmaps_ui.py` (new owner), `streamlit_app.py` (one import, header, route), `src/tab_bar_v4.py` (one nav key), `src/production_manifest.py`, `src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `scripts/validate_architecture.py`, `scripts/test_heatmaps.py`, `.github/workflows/architecture-guard.yml`, and this append-only changelog.
- **What was missing:** No full-history sector comparison heatmaps, no ticker additions/removals for sector analysis, and no reboot-safe Heatmaps watchlist.
- **Root cause:** No Heatmaps production route, isolated historical adjusted-price loader, or associated browser memory.
- **What changed:** Added the 11 official State Street Select Sector SPDR symbols by default; comma/space-separated custom ticker additions, per-ticker deletion and restore-sector control; monthly/annual/rolling-period performance heatmaps with signed continuous red/black/green gradients; first/last available pricing dates; CSV exports for returns and maximum available daily adjusted closes; hourly cached Yahoo/yfinance adjusted daily close history with manual refresh and per-symbol retry. Installed the existing zero-height, four-backup localStorage transport under a distinct hashed Heatmaps key. New browser sessions must finish reading stored state before writing any defaults. Saved view, focus ticker, comparison year and watchlist revisions automatically.
- **Important behavior that must remain:** Use each ticker's actual first available close, not a fictional common inception; no pre-inception calendar returns; monthly/annual compound adjusted daily returns; first/current month/year may be partial. Never claim Yahoo-derived returns are official State Street published NAV performance. Keep watchlist memory browser-profile-local (not cross-device). No market history or state write should touch E*TRADE credentials.
- **Files/features intentionally NOT changed:** `src/terminal_core.py`, `src/theme.py`, GEX owners, Risk Sizing, OAuth, Holdings, Performance, order-entry features, and the 48px tab component HTML.
- **Tests performed:** GitHub Actions Terminal Architecture Guard run `38091368508` passed Python syntax checks for the new files, `scripts/test_heatmaps.py` symbol/history/return/gradient/export coverage, the architecture validator, `scripts/test_tab_layout.py` across enabled top-level tabs, existing broker/order and data-source regressions, workflow syntax, and the deployed Streamlit health endpoint. The frontend-browser check is tracked by the same workflow run.
- **Architecture guard result:** PASS (run `38091368508`).
- **Commit SHAs:** `9b4dd2e` (feature), `8eba217` (route), `5950983` (navigation), `9eb52ff` (monthly return correction), `63f3ebb` (regression in CI), plus intermediate docs/tests commits recorded in GitHub history.
- **Lesson:** Reuse the reboot-hydrated browser memory component for each new independent feature; compound daily returns by calendar year/month and verify group keys are valid pandas groupers; make incomplete/pre-inception coverage explicit.

## 2026-10-10 — Heatmaps rolling-window baseline precision

- **Feature changed:** Heatmaps period-return window anchor; no other feature changed.
- **Exact production files changed:** `src/heatmaps_ui.py`, `scripts/test_heatmaps.py`, and this appended note.
- **What was broken / root cause:** The initial 1W/1M/3M/6M/1Y/3Y/5Y baseline used the last close strictly before the target date, which unintentionally included an extra day's price move when the target date was a trading day.
- **What changed:** Rolling horizons select the last close on OR before the target start date; YTD deliberately keeps the last close before January 1 to include the full calendar year's first trading day. Added an exact 1W boundary regression.
- **Important behavior retained:** MAX uses first available close, calendars compound daily returns, no pre-inception values are invented, settings remain browser-persisted, all other tabs and broker workflows remain unchanged.
- **Files/features NOT changed:** All GEX, Risk, OAuth, Holdings, trade-entry, shared theme/navigation and infrastructure files.
- **Tests performed:** `scripts/test_heatmaps.py` exact-date baseline regression; automatic CI architecture guard, tab-layout, and deployed-health/frontend checks tracked by run `38091532705`.
- **Architecture guard:** Latest CI run `38091532705` (verify conclusion before claiming PASS).
- **Commit SHAs:** `ff2fb857` (production correction), `dd886f70` (regression coverage).
- **Lesson:** A rolling return uses a closing price on its target anchor date when available; YTD is intentionally anchored to the previous year-end close.
